## C215 single-slit-diffraction — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-13-Optics/C215-single-slit-diffraction.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (D1 blind spec → Track P → Track D incl. D8 → Track L → fixes + gates)
- TRACK P: **15 live grid points × 3 quantities = 45 comparisons, 0 mismatches** — a ∈ {25, 50, 100, 162, 300} µm × λ ∈ {400, 550, 700} nm, read from the real sliders through the DOM readouts against an independent Python oracle for θ₁ = asin(λ/a), W = 2L·tan θ₁ and I₀ ∝ a². Secondary-maximum intensities independently re-derived by scanning sinc²(β) (4.719 %, 1.648 %, 0.834 % at β/π = 1.4303, 2.4590, 3.4709) — the sim's `eq4` claim of 0.0472 and card 4's "about 4.7 %" are both right. `__audit` invariant `centralWidthTimesSlitWidthIsConstant` passes (maxDev 2.1e-4 vs tol 2e-3). **The minima-vs-maxima trap is handled correctly everywhere**: `singleSlitMinimum` throws on m = 0, `eq1` is the DARK condition, and no copy or label calls a·sinθ = mλ a maximum · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:3 C:3 R:3 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix L:3 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (5 cards walked from the welcome overlay; the gate holds and resolves on the spot; card numbers 11 mm / 22 mm / 4.7 % all verified against the oracle; Controls Guide walks all 8 steps and the scene canvas survives the hide/unhide cycle at backing:display 1.000; welcome hero leaks nothing — it is an unlabelled abstract dot motif with no axes, no legend and its caption `display:none`)
- **THE AUTO-SCALE CLASS WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.** Driving the slit slider 25 → 300 µm and sampling the light strip's pixel row: the first dark point sits at **21.21 / 10.61 / 5.30 / 2.61 / 1.77 mm** for a = 25 / 50 / 100 / 200 / 300 µm — successive ratios 2.00, 2.00, 2.03, 1.47 against the exact 2, 2, 2, 1.5, so the WIDTH half of the control is pixel-honest and follows 1/a. The BRIGHTNESS half was dead: peak strip luminance was **112.3 at every one of the five widths** while I₀ ∝ a² spans 144:1 across that range, and the strip was captioned "as it appears" — a positive false claim. Fixed below
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the light strip claimed to show the pattern "as it appears" while normalising every configuration to its own peak**, so narrowing the slit widened the pattern without ever dimming it — half of what the star control does was invisible and mis-stated. Encoding absolute brightness would have driven a = 25 µm to (1/12)² → 6 % of peak and erased the very pattern the sim is built around, so the normalisation is kept and made honest: the caption now reads "PATTERN ON SCREEN — scaled to its own peak", the sidebar note says so, and a new readout **Brightness I₀ ∝ a²** carries the true central brightness against the a = 100 µm default · verified: 0.06 / 0.25 / 1.00 / 2.62 / 9.00 × at a = 25 / 50 / 100 / 162 / 300 µm, matching (a/100)² exactly
- FIXED [P1][FUNC/LAYER] **↻ Reset did not reset anything.** `onReset` re-read the sliders into the closure instead of returning them to their authored defaults, so Reset was a no-op on the parameters — while the Controls Guide told the student "Restores a = 100 µm and λ = 550 nm". The shell contract puts that restore in `onReset` precisely because its restart-on-parameter-change watcher snapshots the controls around the call and puts a dragged value back. Defaults are now captured at boot and written back · verified: 37 µm / 662 nm → Reset → 100 µm / 550 nm / W 11.00 mm / I₀ 1.00 ×, while a slider drag still keeps its dragged value (37 µm / 662 nm held across the watcher's restart)
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **card 3 taught the cancellation with the argument that does not work.** It read "light from one edge arrives exactly one wavelength behind light from the other edge — they cancel". Two rays a full λ apart are *in phase*; they reinforce. The NCERT argument is the halves pairing, and this is the most-confused step of the most-confused result in the chapter. Rewritten: split the slit in two, every point in the top half has a partner λ/2 behind it in the bottom half, the pairs cancel. Re-trimmed after the guided-inquiry verifier flagged 99 words against its 90-word budget · verified: verifier PASS, and the card voiceover reads the corrected text (it is generated from the card DOM)
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the "W = … mm" label was drawn on top of the intensity curve's central peak** — the red width readout and the apex of the green sinc² occupied the same pixels at every parameter combination, worst at wide central bands. The curve band now starts 34 px lower and the measurement lives in that gap as a **double-headed arrow spanning the two first minima with the value above it** — clear of the peak, and directly under the band it measures (Mayer contiguity). The arrow is suppressed when the minima fall outside the ±25 mm screen, where a clamped span would misstate W · verified at both viewports and at a = 25 µm/λ = 700 nm (off-screen case: label + "first minima → off screen", no arrow, no dashed lines)
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the DOM legend pill clipped the strip's caption at every window size** (pill bottom at canvas y = 73, caption ascender at y = 68) — "PATTERN ON SCREEN…" was struck through, and at 1024×768 the pill also cut into the apparatus label. The strip start moved from `scT+26` to `scT+40` · verified: caption ascender now at y = 81, clear of the pill, at 1500×950 and 1024×768
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the legend was dark-on-dark in light theme** — `.legend` hard-codes `background:rgba(6,14,17,.72)` while its text follows `--text`, which flips to `rgb(15,23,42)`: computed contrast **2.16:1** against the composited `rgb(74,80,83)` fill, i.e. the diagram's only key was unreadable in one of two themes. Light-theme override added · verified: `rgba(248,250,252,.92)` fill with `#0f172a` text under `body.light-theme`
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the dark fringes carried no order index**, so `a·sinθ = mλ` — the equation in the Formal panel and in card 3 — could not be read off the picture, and the fact that the central band is exactly *twice* the spacing of every other band was left to be inferred. The first minima are now labelled **m = 1** on the outer side of each dashed line (so the pair never collides however narrow the band gets), the m = 2…5 minima get short dim ticks at the axis, and the legend key reads "Minima (m = 1, 2, …)" — which also glosses `m` on first appearance · verified at a = 25 → 300 µm: labels legible and non-overlapping at both viewports
- FIXED [P2][D8] **the apparatus was three unnamed abstractions** — a dark bar, some vertical lines and a divider. Added "opaque barrier with a slit" over the barrier and a rotated "the screen (L = 1.00 m)" down the divider column, which is what tells a first-time reader that the right-hand panel *is* the screen. Both placements were then moved once more after the 1024×768 screenshot showed them under the legend pill and across the Huygens arcs; the arcs' reach was trimmed from `+40` to `+26` px so they stop short of the I/I₀ axis labels · verified at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- FIXED [P2][D8] **eight canvas strings were 11px**, below the 12px floor (axis ticks, axis title, I/I₀, the 1.0 and 0 gridline labels, the strip caption, the off-screen note, "schematic — not to scale"); all raised to 12px, and the legend from 11px to 12px
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] **the plane wavefronts were nearly invisible in light theme** — a saturated spectral colour at α 0.55 over `#f8fafc`. Light theme now draws them at 0.55× RGB and α 0.85 (arcs 0.45); the strip and the legend dot keep the true hue in both themes
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] the Controls Guide's readouts step listed two readouts; it now names the third, and the Formal panel's `eq1` label changed from "First minimum (single slit)" to "Dark fringes — minima, not maxima (single slit)", since the equation shown covers all m and the label named only m = 1 next to the syllabus's single most-confused sign result
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — draw the half-slit cancellation.** The mechanism is asserted in card 3 and nowhere visible: the Huygens arcs are decorative, no θ is drawn, no path difference is drawn, and nothing links the aperture to a point on the screen. Sketch: reclaim the left panel (it currently spends 42 % of the canvas on repeating plane-wave lines that carry no varying information), draw two rays from the slit edges to a draggable point on the screen with the angle θ marked and the path difference a·sinθ shown as a live bar against a λ ruler; at the first minimum split the aperture into two shaded halves and animate a top-half point pairing with its λ/2 partner below, the pair's phasors cancelling. That converts "squeeze a wave and it spreads" from a rule into a watchable event. (2) **a pinned ghost pattern** — "Pin this pattern" freezing the current strip + curve as a dimmed overlay so narrow-vs-wide is a visible ratio rather than a memory comparison; the sim's one concept *is* a comparison and the student currently holds the before-case in their head. (3) **a true-brightness toggle** switching the strip and the curve between "scaled to its own peak" and absolute I ∝ a²·sinc²β on a fixed axis, which would let the student *see* the 144:1 dimming the new I₀ readout can only state. (4) **a screen-distance L control** (0.5–3 m): W = 2λL/a is in the Formal panel and on card 3, and the L half of it is untestable — it is also what separates the ANGULAR width λ/a (L-independent) from the linear width, a standard JEE distinction. (5) **reach the a ≈ λ regime** — the slider bottoms out at a = 25 µm ≈ 45λ, so the limit where the first minimum runs to 90° and the pattern washes out entirely (and the a < λ case where there is *no* minimum) cannot be reached; needs a sinθ axis or an auto-ranging screen, not just a wider slider, since ±25 mm is fixed. (6) **the double-slit envelope overlay** — showing that this sinc² *is* the envelope of the double-slit fringes, the connection JEE examines most often and the natural bridge to the sibling C213
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the slit gap is drawn linear in a but hugely exaggerated against the cm-scale apparatus — disclosed on-canvas as "schematic — not to scale". The I/I₀ curve is peak-normalised by construction, which is the NCERT plot and is now stated twice on screen. `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)` at the top of `render()` is assigned to an unused `cs` and left alone (dead, harmless, and the theme is read correctly from `document.body`, matching where the toggle lands the class). Shell template chrome below the D8 13px bar (Listen 9px, Auto 10px, panel h3 11px) is platform-shared CSS and was not changed in one file. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (0 occurrences — all sites are literal px plus a concrete family stack), theme read from `documentElement` while the class lands on `body` (render reads `body`, and the hero reads both), pause-desync (this build ships no Play/Speed control; the rAF loop renders every frame and a slider change routes through the shell's restart → `onReset` → `render`), predict-gate leak (the single gate asks what happens when a is *halved*; nothing on screen pre-commit shows the 50 µm case, and the welcome hero is unlabelled), temporal aliasing (wavefronts drift 40 px/s at 13–28 px spacing), affine/clamped encodings (the dashed minima markers are *suppressed*, not clamped, when the true minimum leaves the ±25 mm screen). The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404
- Gates: syntax ✓ (9/9 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 8 steps, guided-inquiry 5 cards) · user-version-eval ✓ (exit 0) · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (repo `canvas-check.mjs` enumerates a hardcoded QM sim list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly against the pre-fix build: `#scene` 1150×771 / 1150×771 at 1500×950 and 674×550 / 674×550 at 1024×768, ratio 1.000/1.000, drift 0.0000 pre→post, and unchanged across the Controls Guide's hide/unhide of the scene) · copyright ✓ · `__audit` ratchet unchanged (pass, 2.1e-4 vs tol 2e-3)

## C183 magnetic-flux-and-faradays-law — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-12-EMI-AC-and-EM-Waves/C183-magnetic-flux-and-faradays-law.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)

Plus a 10-point ε grid under oscillation against an analytic dΦ/dt, and three lifecycle probes. Special points confirmed: **a stationary magnet at d = 0 with N = 4, S = 100 %, A = 100 % (Φ = 4.000 Wb*, the largest flux the sim can make) gives ε = 0, I = 0, brightness 0 % exactly** — the JEE-critical claim, machine-true, not merely small; the magnet driven *through* the coil never makes Φ negative (Φ peaks at 0.719 against an analytic 0.750 at the sampled frame) and ε flips sign exactly at the moment of passage (mean −0.598 approaching, +1.151 receding). **Lenz handedness verified analytically against the drawing's oblique projection**, not assumed: the right ellipse half is drawn as the far half and canvas-y is inverted, so α = ePhase + π, electrons circulate along +x̂ when ePhase rises, conventional current along −x̂ — and an approaching N pole (Φ_x rising) drives current < 0 → ePhase rising → conventional current along −x̂, opposing the increase. Correct · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:3 C:3 R:4 L:3 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:3 R:3 L:2 G:2 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (deck walked card 1→5 from the welcome overlay; both new gates hold Next disabled until a choice and resolve on the spot; Reset → card 1 + magnet parked clear + readouts re-folded + all sliders at default; Controls Guide walks all 13 steps with every callout on screen; free exploration expands the readouts)
- FIXED [P0][DEMO/FUNC] **the whole canvas rendered in dark-theme ink in light theme.** `themeColors()` read the custom properties from `document.documentElement`, but the shell's theme button lands `light-theme` on `<body>` — so in light mode the canvas kept `--text #e2e8f0` and `--muted #cbd5e1` against a `#f8fafc` plot surface. Reads from `document.body` now · verified: 17.06:1 and 9.90:1, label pixel luminance 60–63 on a ~250 ground
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the ε display filter distorted the one proportionality this sim exists to teach, and made the answer depend on the Speed setting.** `emf += (emfRaw−emf)·min(1, dt/TAU_LR)` is a step-size-dependent one-pole with τ = 60 ms; against the analytic peak |ε| it ran −0.2 % at 0.1 Hz but **−17.7 % at 1.5 Hz** (1.305 vs 1.585), so sweeping the oscillation slider 0.1 → 1.5 Hz (15×) moved ε only 12.4×, and because the coefficient saturates at 1 once dt ≥ τ the same configuration read **1.275 at Speed 0.25× and 1.493 at Speed 4× — 17 % of answer moved by a playback control**. Now `emf += (emfRaw−emf)·(1−exp(−dt/TAU_LR))` with τ = 20 ms · verified: ε∝f residual falls to −4.6 % at 1.5 Hz and ≤ −0.7 % at 0.5 Hz; ε is exactly ∝ N (0.2624 / 0.5248 / 1.0497 for N = 1/2/4), exactly ∝ A (0.2100 / 0.5248 / 1.0498) and exactly ∝ strength (0.1750 / 0.5248 / 0.6999) against the oracle; Speed spread 1.5138 / 1.5116 / 1.4738 = 2.7 %, was 17.1 %; the stationary case is still exactly 0; readout roughness (mean |2nd difference| / peak) is 0.34 %, so nothing became jittery

Terse claim-only choices, every answer resolving on the spot, zero UI choreography. `onStep` re-staged to match, and it now **opens the Readouts panel and scrolls it into the sidebar's view from card 3 onward** (and on entry to free exploration), while re-folding it at cards 1–2 for gate hygiene · verified: full card walk, gates, feedback, Finish, Reset

Added a sim-local `@media(min-width:1001px) and (max-width:1240px)` wrap rule (the verbatim shell block is untouched, and ≥1240 px is byte-identical) · verified: header scrollWidth 1024, Play at 929–1004, all nine controls hit-testable, canvas refits from y=77 to y=110
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the brightness meter pinned silently at 100 %.** `brightness = min(1,(I/0.7)²)` saturates at |I| = 0.7, and the reachable range goes far past it — measured peak |I| = 1.02 at N = 4 (saturated 31 % of the cycle), 1.36 at S = 100 %/A = 100 % (42 %), **6.96 at N = 4, S = 100 %, A = 100 %, f = 1.5 Hz (86 % of the cycle)** — so over the top half of three sliders the meter reads a constant "100 %" while ε and I keep growing, and the controls look dead. The readout now reads `100% · maxed` when clipped · verified: both strings observed across a 2.5 s saturated cycle. (Defaults peak at 57 % and never saturate — the default demonstration is unaffected)
- FIXED [P2][D8] **novice legibility of the scene.** The bulb — the sim's whole payoff — was an unlabelled 16 px circle; the magnet had only "drag me"; canvas labels sat at the 12 px floor; the legend read the bare symbols "field B" and "electrons". Now: `bulb` printed beside the glass, `bar magnet — drag me`, `coil · N = 2 loops` (live with the slider), all at 13 px; legend reads "magnetic field B (fainter = weaker)" — the arrow grid encodes |B| in opacity at a fixed 11 px length, which was nowhere stated — and "electrons in the wire". Sidebar rows put words before symbols (`Induced EMF ε`, not `ε = −dΦ/dt (EMF)`), with the four defining formulas moved intact to the note directly beneath · verified: no label clips at any of 4 slider-extreme configurations at either viewport
- FIXED [P2][D8] two regressions the light-theme fix exposed and one it caused. The shell's `.legend` hard-codes `background:rgba(6,14,17,.72)` while its ink follows `--text`; with the canvas palette corrected the pill became dark-on-dark at **2.20:1** in light theme (it had been legible only by accident, on the dark ink of the bug) → light-theme override added, 17.06:1. The longer legend text then overlapped the `.plot-title` at 1024×768 (legend 186–673 px against a title ending at 373) → `max-width:calc(100% − 390px)` lets it wrap, measured clear at 389 px. The longer sidebar labels wrapped the stat rows onto two lines → shortened, all four rows measured single-line
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a live Φ(t) and ε(t) strip under the scene.** ε = −dΦ/dt is the title of the concept and the derivative relation appears nowhere on screen: the student reads two numbers and has to imagine the slope between them. The welcome hero already draws exactly this trace, so the sim advertises a representation it does not contain. Sketch: a ~110 px band below the plot-box (or the bottom quarter of the same canvas) with a scrolling 6 s window, Φ(t) and ε(t) on twin axes sharing one time cursor, a zero rule on the ε axis, and the interval where Φ is flat shaded — so "flat Φ ⇒ ε = 0", "steeper Φ ⇒ taller ε" and "Φ falling ⇒ ε changes sign" are three *pictures* instead of three assertions, and cards 2, 3 and 4 all become readable off one frame. (2) **coil tilt θ, restoring Φ = B·A·cos θ.** The formal panel states the general flux law and then immediately annihilates it with θ = 0; there is no angle control at all, so the projection half of the definition — and the rotating-coil result Φ = BA cos ωt, ε = NBAω sin ωt that JEE examines directly — is unreachable. Sketch: a 0–90° slider rotating the drawn ellipses about the vertical, foreshortening the drawn aperture by cos θ and multiplying Φ by cos θ; θ = 90° then gives Φ = 0 under a large B, the cleanest possible demonstration that flux is a projection. (3) **draw the current direction, not only the drift.** Lenz's reversal is the payoff of cards 3 and 5 and is carried solely by which way amber dots crawl round a near-edge-on ellipse. Sketch: two conventional-current chevrons on the front arc of each loop that flip with sign(ε), plus an induced-B arrow inside the coil drawn opposite to the change in Φ. (4) **field lines that thread the coil.** ~450 uniform-length arrows fill the canvas as texture and none is identified as "the flux through the coil"; drawing 5–7 continuous lines from the N pole, colouring the segments that pass through the loop aperture and printing the count makes Φ *countable* — the NCERT picture. (5) **q = ΔΦ/R.** The standard corollary that the total induced charge depends on the flux change and not on how long it took is one integrator away and is exactly what this sim's structure demonstrates for free: slow sweep vs fast sweep, different ε, identical q
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the remaining −4.6 % in ε∝f at 1.5 Hz is the one-frame backward difference `−(Φ−Φ_prev)/dt` sampled at 60 fps (40 frames per cycle) and is intrinsic to a per-frame derivative. `EMF_CLAMP = 12` is a sanity guard never reached in the reachable range (largest measured peak |ε| = 6.96). The magnet-strength slider changes the arrow field only through opacity — the count is constant at 453 and the length fixed at 11 px, and raising the alpha floor to 0.18 removes only 6 arrows — but that is a conventional direction-field encoding, now disclosed in the legend, and thinning it is folded into enhancement (4) rather than done on taste. The header-wrap fix is file-local: the same over-subscription exists fleet-wide (measured on C185 at 1024×768, where `btn-gi`/`btn-cg` overlap `shell-maximize`/`toggle-formal` instead of overflowing) and deserves a shell-level fix. Shell chrome below the D8 13 px bar (Listen 9 px, Auto 10 px, panel `h3` 11 px, `.plot-title` 11 px, legend 11 px, `.control-range` 12 px) is platform-shared template CSS and was not forked in one file. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (both `ctx.font` sites are literal px — `getPropertyValue('--font-sans')` returns a resolved family list, confirmed by reading back `ctx.font` as `600 13px DMSansUser, "DM Sans", …`); pause-desync (the shell's rAF loop runs unconditionally and `onFrame` always redraws, and `pointermove` adds an immediate repaint while paused — canvas hashes differ at every slider value); auto-scale cancelling a control (the world→px scale is fixed at `min(w/145, h/64)` and loop radius is `R_MAX·√(A/100)·s`, so drawn *area* is exactly ∝ A); affine/clamped encodings (glow radius and alpha are both ∝ brightness from zero, and the clip is now disclosed); temporal aliasing (electron phase advances ≤0.47 rad/frame against a 0.785 rad dot spacing, even at Speed 4× and 1.5 Hz); Speed changing the answer (fixed above, 2.7 % residual); `data-no-reset` — no view-only buttons exist outside the shell. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404

## C206 thin-lens-formula-and-lensmakers-equation — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-13-Optics/C206-thin-lens-formula-and-lensmakers-equation.html` · jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L) · blind D1 spec written before reading the implementation
- TRACK P: 133 grid points (u × R × n = 6×4×4 full cross product, plus u = f and u = 2f for all 16 (R,n) pairs, plus 6 extreme cross-combinations), read from the LIVE page. **0 numeric mismatches** against an independent NCERT-Cartesian oracle (f = R/2(n−1); v = fd/(d−f); m = −v/d) for f, v, m, image kind and the u display. The 4 rows the harness still prints are a float tie at |m| = 1 exactly at u = 2f, where the oracle's `1+2e-16` asks for ", enlarged" and the sim correctly prints no size descriptor — the sim is the more correct of the two. `__audit.at` matches the oracle to the last bit; `__audit.run()` passes both invariants. No NaN/∞ leaks, u = f handled as `atFocalPoint` rather than a huge number. browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:4 R:3 L:4 G:4 N:4 (was V:3 C:3 R:2 L:3 G:2 N:2 pre-fix)
- FIXED [P0][PHYS] **the sim claimed the NCERT sign convention and used the opposite one.** Header "· NCERT sign convention", plot subtitle "u > 0 in front … m = −v/u", formal label "Thin lens formula (NCERT)" and the rendered equation `1/u + 1/v = 1/f, m = −v/u` are the legacy real-is-positive convention — which is the *mirror* formula's shape. NCERT Class 12 §9.4.1/9.5.2 is Cartesian: u < 0 for a real object, **1/v − 1/u = 1/f, m = v/u**. A JEE student pairing this sim's rule with the Cartesian u they are taught everywhere else puts the image on the wrong side with the wrong magnification sign. Converted the whole presentation layer to true Cartesian; the shared `Engine.rays` module is untouched and still called with a positive distance, which is exact because only u flips: u_c = −u_rp ⟹ 1/v − 1/u_c = 1/v + 1/u_rp = 1/f and m_c = v/u_c = −v/u_rp = m_rp, so **v, f and m are numerically identical in the two conventions**. Changed: eq1 KaTeX, the eq-note (now also states the lens-vs-mirror minus explicitly), the plot subtitle, the u readout and its range endpoints, the on-canvas `u = −0.400 m`, the four note-text strings (now |u| where magnitudes are compared), all five inquiry cards, the Info modal and the Reset callout · verified: 133/133 grid points read `u = −<d> m`; v/f/m/kind unchanged at every point (the whole grid re-run pre- and post-fix)
- FIXED [P0][PHYS] **the Controls Guide stated the focal length as the power.** Step 5 read "Sets R. Focal length is 2(n − 1)/R" — at R = 0.20, n = 1.50 that is 5 (m⁻¹) while f = 0.20 m. Now "Lens-maker: 1/f = 2(n − 1)/R, so a fatter radius lengthens f" · verified: controls-tutorial verifier passes (the first rewrite tripped its ≤18-word budget and was re-trimmed)
- FIXED [P1][PHYS/DEMO] **phantom F and 2F markers pinned to the canvas border.** `drawLabel` clamps any caption into the canvas, so whenever 2F fell outside the framed extent the sim still painted "2F" hard against the left and right edges with no dot under it — at u = 0.10 m, f = 0.20 m the extent is 0.276 m while 2f = 0.40 m, so both 2F captions sat ~1.5× too far out on the axis (screenshotted pre-fix in dark and light). Markers and captions are now skipped when the anchor is outside `[marginX, w−marginX]` · verified: at u = 0.10 / 0.20 (f = 0.20) only the two F dots and their labels remain, each with its dot
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the object walked clean off the canvas.** `extent = min(max(|u|,|v|,1.2f)·1.15, 8f)` applied the 8f cap AFTER folding in |u|, so every setting with |u| > 8f lost the object arrow, its `u =` dimension bracket and the near F/2F off the left edge — measured at **8 of the 133 grid points**, including (u = −1.20, R = 0.20, n = 1.90) → objX = −120 px on a 1150 px canvas, (u = −1.20, R = 0.10, n = 1.90) → −816 px, and (u = −0.80, R = 0.10, n = 1.70) → −146 px; the screenshot shows the "object" caption clamped at the border with no arrow beneath it and rays entering from nowhere. The cap now applies only to the runaway image term: `max(|u|, min(|v|, 8f), 1.2f)·1.15` · verified: **off-canvas object cases 8 → 0** across the same 133-point grid, and the near-u = f framing the 8f cap existed for is unchanged (extent 1.84 m at u = −0.205, f = 0.20)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the third construction ray was suppressed for |u| < f — the magnifying-glass case the sim is built around.** An `if(u > f)` guard dropped ray 3 entirely below f while the legend kept advertising "F → parallel" and cards 4 and 5 both live there; the student saw a two-ray construction and a dead legend row. The geometry was already correct there and unused: probed at u = 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.15 m (f = 0.20) the ray's height at the lens equals the image tip height to **< 0.5 px** (30.94 vs 30.94, 152.46 vs 152.46, −333.62 vs −333.62) — the ray on the F₁ line emerges parallel at exactly the virtual image height. Guard removed, and the emergent ray given the same dashed back-extension to the image tip that rays 1 and 2 already had when v < 0 · verified: all three rays and all three dashed extensions now converge on the virtual image tip at u = −0.10 m in both themes
- FIXED [P1][LAYER/DEMO] **all three predict gates were pre-answered on screen** (the class reported-and-not-fixed in C198/C194/C192/C037). At card 1 — gated, Next disabled, no choice clicked — the sidebar already read `v = 0.400 m`, `m = −1.00`, "real, inverted", the note-text read "Image at 2f, same size, inverted" *verbatim the correct choice*, and the canvas drew the image arrow at 2F labelled "image" with `v = +0.400 m / m = −1.00`. Card 3 showed `∞ / ∞ / rays parallel — no image` and an on-canvas "image at ∞" against a choice reading "the image is at infinity"; card 4's note read "virtual image on the same side, upright and enlarged … it cannot land on a screen" against a choice reading "you see it fine; it just can't be caught on a screen", and the card's own body stated the answer before asking. A `gateHeld()` test (active `.inq-step[data-gate]` without `data-answered`, inquiry zone visible via `offsetParent`) now stops the rays AT THE LENS, withholds the image arrow and its label, and shows `v = ?`, `m = ?`, kind `?` with "Commit your prediction to send the rays through the lens." The incident rays, object, lens, F and 2F, f and u stay drawn — the student sees the full setup and nothing of the outcome. Card 4's body was rewritten to pose rather than state. Nothing is `display:none`'d and no geometry moves · verified: 5-card walk — cards 1/3/4 read `v:? m:? kind:?` pre-commit and full values post-commit, cards 2 and 5 (ungated) always live, Finish / Controls Guide / free exploration / lecture all show the complete scene
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **a ghost image label where no image was.** At u = −0.210 m (f = 0.20) v = +4.200 m, so imgX = 3214 px on a 1150 px canvas — nothing drawn, yet `drawLabel` pinned "image" in the bottom-right corner. Replaced with an explicit edge chip "image off-scale: v = +4.200 m, m = −20.00", and the same test catches the vertical overflow at |u| → f⁻ · verified by screenshot at u = −0.210
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the legend pill was dark-on-dark in light theme** — `.legend` hard-codes `background:rgba(6,14,17,.72)` while its text follows `--text` (`#0f172a` in light). Added the canonical light-theme override used by the other 3 JEE sims that hit this · verified by screenshot
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the readout labels lost their spaces**: `.stat-label{display:flex}` made the text node and the `<em>` siblings flex items and dropped the whitespace, so the panel read "Focal lengthf", "Image distancev", "Magnificationm" in every screenshot. `gap:4px` · verified
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the lens was never named.** Every other object in the scene carries a caption ("object", "image", "principal axis", "F", "2F", "u = …") but the thing at the centre was a bare teal double-arrowed line — the one glyph a first-encounter class-11 student cannot decode. Now captioned "convex (converging) lens" above the existing `f = …` label
- FIXED [P2][D8] **colour was the only channel separating the three construction rays**, with the key in a corner pill rather than on the rays (Mayer contiguity). Each incident ray now carries its legend number on the ray itself, placed on the side away from the axis and suppressed on segments under 80 px; the legend rows carry the matching numerals
- FIXED [P2][D8] canvas captions below the 12px floor ("principal axis", `f = …`) raised from 11px to 12px
- FIXED [P2][D8] **the image caption overprinted the plot title** at high magnification — at u = −0.10 m the virtual tip sits 7 px from the canvas top, so `drawLabel`'s clamp parked "virtual image" on top of the "OPTICAL BENCH — NCERT sign…" row. The caption now flips inside the scene with an ↑ / ↓ marker when its preferred position would leave the drawing area
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] **the welcome layer answered two gated cards.** Blurb: "Walk the object toward f and the real image races to infinity — then reappears virtual on the object side"; narration line 2: "…Inside f it reappears virtual, upright and magnified" — cards 3 and 4 ask exactly those. Both rewritten to pose the question; the hero caption likewise
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a diverging (concave) lens.** The sim hard-wires an equiconvex lens (`lensMaker(n, R, −R)`) so f > 0 always and half the JEE syllabus — the concave lens, whose image is virtual, erect and diminished for *every* object distance, and the sign rule that produces it — is unreachable at every one of the 133 grid points. Sketch: a two-button Convex / Concave selector in the Controls panel flipping the radii to `lensMaker(n, −R, +R)`; `Engine.rays.thinLens` already accepts f < 0 and returns the right data, `drawLabel`-based F/2F markers already handle either side, the lens glyph's arrowheads reverse, and one extra inquiry card ("predict the image for a concave lens at |u| = 2f") turns the contrast into a prediction. (2) **independent R₁ and R₂** so plano-convex (R₂ = ∞) and meniscus lenses exist and the lens-maker's *two-surface* structure stops being hidden behind the `= 2(n−1)/R` shortcut the equation row already prints. (3) **a v-vs-u graph beside the bench**, cursor-synced to the object, with the asymptote at u = −f drawn: "the image races to infinity" is currently a sequence of positions the student must hold in memory, and the hyperbola is the JEE-examined object. (4) **a scale lock / metre ruler.** The bench auto-fits, so raising n from 1.30 to 1.50 (f 0.333 → 0.200 m) moves the F dot *outward* in pixels even though f halved — relative to the object it is honest, but "denser glass shortens f" cannot be seen as a length change without a fixed reference. (5) **a screen at the image plane** that lights up for a real image and stays dark for a virtual one — the sim's declared misconception target is argued in card 4's text but never demonstrated.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the `★` on the u control is a house convention shared by 12 JEE sims and was not redefined in one file; the shared Feedback pill overlaps the last sidebar row at 1024×768 and is platform chrome; the vertical overflow of a strongly magnified image is inherent to keeping the image-height growth pixel-truthful (the ↑ marker now discloses it). Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (all 12 `ctx.font` sites are literal px; `getCSSVar('--font-sans')` returns a resolved family list), canvas theme read from `documentElement` while the class lands on `body` (`isLight` reads `document.body`; `getCSSVar` is used only for font families, which are theme-independent), pause-desync (`timeless:true`, no transport — every repaint is slider-driven), auto-scale cancelling a control (all seven min/def/max pixel signatures differ; F-to-object ratio tracks f/|u| exactly), affine/clamped encodings, temporal aliasing. Console is clean in both themes at both viewports apart from the harness's `/favicon.ico` 404
- Gates: syntax ✓ (7/7 inline blocks, `node --check`) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (guided-inquiry 5 cards, controls-tutorial 9 steps, welcome-overlay) · user-version-eval ✓ (`all 1 builds pass`) · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (the repo script enumerates a hardcoded QM list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly against the pre-fix copy: `scene` 1.000/1.000 in both, drift 0.0000) · copyright ✓
- browser-verified: Track P yes · Track D yes · Track D8 yes (1500×950 and 1024×768, dark and light) · Track L yes (welcome → all three modes, 5-card walk with both gate states, Reset, Maximize, Controls Guide 9 steps, theme toggle)
- S3: re-push required for this file.

## C198 displacement-current — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-12-EMI-AC-and-EM-Waves/C198-displacement-current.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)
- TRACK P: **29 live grid points, 0 mismatches.** 9 through `window.__audit.at` (I ∈ {0.05, 0.25, 0.50, 1.00, 1.25, 2.00} A at A = 0.01 m², d = 1 mm, plus three (A, d) pairs at I = 0.7 A) and 20 through the real slider reading the DOM tallies (I ∈ {0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00, 1.25, 1.50, 1.75, 2.00} A × Maxwell {OFF, ON}), against an independent Node oracle for Q = It, E = Q/(ε₀A), Φ_E = Q/ε₀, dΦ_E/dt = I/ε₀ and I_d = ε₀·dΦ_E/dt. **The central identity is exact to machine precision: I_d − I = 1.1e−16 A over the whole slider range, and I_d is bit-identical across a 50× plate-area range and a 4× separation range** — the flat disc reads I, the bulging bag reads 0 with Ampère alone and exactly I with the Maxwell term, so the two Amperian surfaces give the same enclosed current at every grid point. ε₀ = 8.8541878128e−12 (CODATA 2018) is the engine constant, used once. No NaN/Infinity, no integrator (the model is closed-form), Speed 4× leaves every readout bit-identical · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:3 R:4 L:3 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:2 R:3 L:3 G:2 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes, plus maximize
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (5 cards walked from the welcome overlay with every card's instruction followed literally; the single gate holds Next disabled and now leaks nothing pre-commit; Reset → card 1 + I = 0.50 A + Maxwell OFF; Finish collapses the zone and reveals everything; Controls Guide walks all 10 steps and lifts the staging)
- **THE SIM DOES SHOW THE PARADOX BEFORE RESOLVING IT** — Maxwell's term boots OFF, cards 1–3 live entirely in the contradiction, and the resolution is a student action on card 4. That structure was sound and was not changed; what was broken was that the screen gave the answer away before the question was asked, and that the resolution could not actually be exercised
- FIXED [P0][LAYER] **the predict-gate leak carried as reported-not-fixed.** Card 2 asks "can the disc's tally and the bag's tally agree?" (correct answer: no, they differ by exactly I) while the screen printed, pre-commit: a 21.6px canvas banner reading `disc = 0.50 A ≠ bag = 0.00 A ✗ Ampère alone contradicts itself`, the canvas tallies `disc: 0.50 A` / `bag: 0.00 A`, and the whole sidebar panel (`Flat disc 0.50 A`, `Bulging bag 0.00 A`, `✗ disagree by 0.50 A`, and a note spelling out "With Maxwell's term OFF, they don't"). The sim's own code comment asserted "this sim stages no reveals". A `talliesShown()` test (inquiry active AND step ≤ 1 AND the gate card lacks `data-answered`) now stages exactly the numbers: the panel is `display:none`, the two surfaces keep their NAMES (`flat disc` / `bulging bag` — better labelling than before, D8 #1), and the banner states only the PREMISE under test ("Ampère: every surface bounded by this loop must enclose the same current"). Everything returns on the first click of any choice, right or wrong; the controls guide and free exploration lift it outright · verified: `panel-tallies` staged at steps 0 and 1, unstaged the instant a choice is clicked, at both viewports
- FIXED [P0][LAYER] **the welcome layer leaked the same answer twice more.** The blurb read "one Ampèrian loop gives two different answers, until Maxwell's term" and the narration's second line read "the enclosed current comes out different on each — until Maxwell's term counts the growing electric flux …" — card 2's answer and card 4's resolution, both on the title screen before the student has seen the apparatus. Both rewritten to set the scene and state Ampère's claim without settling it
- FIXED [P0][PHYS/DEMO] **the welcome hero drew a chain of charge dots marching straight across the empty gap** — the sim's own Info modal names "displacement current is a real current of something moving across the gap" as the misconception it exists to kill, and card 5 says "Nothing. No charge, no matter." The hero pictured the opposite, and the (hidden) hero caption said so in words: "continuing across the empty gap". The dot chain now stops dead at the plate and resumes on the far wire at the same spacing; only the E arrows occupy the gap. Caption rewritten to match
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **Maxwell's term could be switched ON but never OFF.** `#maxwellToggle` is a plain button, so the shell's capture-phase `onParamButton` treated it as a parameter and ran `restartWithCurrentParams()` → `onReset()` FIRST, which set `maxwellOn = false` and repainted the button "OFF"; the sim's own handler then ran and flipped it back to true. Measured: OFF → ON → ON → ON. The button carries `data-no-reset` now (the canonical shield — this toggle changes how the bag's enclosure is ACCOUNTED, not what the apparatus does, so it should never restart the run) · verified: OFF → ON → OFF → ON, and the E field no longer collapses to zero each time the term is toggled
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **moving the current slider silently switched Maxwell's term back off.** `restartWithCurrentParams()` snapshots `simParams()` — inputs and selects only — then replays `onReset()`, which cleared `maxwellOn`; a button's state cannot be restored that way. Card 4's instruction is literally "Sweep the current slider again. Read both tallies. They agree — not approximately, exactly — at every setting", and following it produced `✗ disagree by 1.50 A`. `onReset()` now clears the term only on a genuine ↻ Reset (detected by a capture-phase listener on `#shell-reset`), not on a parameter restart · verified: with the term ON, I swept 0.05 → 2.00 A across 5 settings leaves it ON and the tallies agreeing at every one; ↻ Reset still restores I = 0.50 A and OFF
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **pause-desync on both controls.** `draw()` ran only from `onFrame`, so while paused the sidebar moved and the canvas did not: dragging I to 2.00 A left the scene byte-identical (md5 of the canvas dataURL unchanged) still printing `I = 0.50 A`, and toggling Maxwell left the sidebar reading `✓ agree exactly` beside a red canvas banner reading `≠ … Ampère alone contradicts itself`. Both listeners now repaint · verified: canvas hash changes on both gestures while paused, at 1500×950
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the drawn E field encoded the CURRENT, not the accumulated charge.** `chargeVis = min(1, tCharge · state.I · 0.6)` multiplies elapsed time by the *present* value of I, so the field length tracked I rather than ∫I dt — E = Q/(ε₀A) and Q is an integral. Replaced by a proper accumulator `qVis = min(1, qVis + dt·I·0.6)`: monotone, so lowering the current slows the growth and never shrinks the field. The old `tCharge > 4` freeze went with it (it also meant the label "E growing" became false after ~3 s at the default, while the bag tally kept reading I — i.e. the visual said dΦ_E/dt → 0 exactly where the sim claims I_d = ε₀dΦ_E/dt = I) · verified: arrow length strictly non-decreasing within a run; measured amber arrow length 1 s after a restart is 0 / 0 / 7 / 8 / 128 px for I = 0.05 / 0.25 / 0.50 / 1.0 / 2.0 A, so the slider drives real pixels with the right sense
- FIXED [P1][PHYS/D8] **the gap label was a bare formal fragment that became untrue, and the display cap was undisclosed.** `E growing → dΦ_E/dt` gave a novice an unglossed Φ with no name, asserted growth that stopped once the display saturated, and overlapped the `bag:` tally by 3–4 px (measured; visibly smeared at both viewports). Replaced by two lines — `electric field E — growing between the plates` and `electric flux rate dΦ_E/dt = 5.6×10¹⁰ V·m/s  (arrow length capped)` — which names the quantity in words before symbols, puts the real SI number of the *rate* on screen (the thing I_d is built from, and the thing that genuinely is constant), and discloses the not-to-scale cap the skill requires · verified: 0 label-box overlaps at 1500×950 and 1024×768
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the two plates were unlabelled white bars.** Added `capacitor plates · plate area A = 0.01 m², gap d = 1 mm` above the gap — the apparatus is now nameable from the pixels and its two fixed dimensions are on screen, which also makes the printed flux rate checkable (dΦ_E/dt = I/ε₀). The step-5 "nothing crosses the gap" annotation moved up 22 px to clear it
- FIXED [P1][D8] **every scene colour was dark-theme-only, at ~1.5:1 on the light canvas.** The canvas correctly re-read `body.light-theme` for its background, wire and plates, but the amber E field, pink disc, blue bag, grey loop, ± charges and the green/red verdict were hard-coded pastels; the 18px amber label on `#f1f5f9` measured ≈1.5:1. A `--c-disc/--c-bag/--c-efield/--c-loop/--c-pos/--c-neg/--c-yes/--c-no` palette now has light-theme darkened values, is read back from `document.body` on every frame, and is the SAME source the legend swatches, the sidebar tally dots and the card-1 colour chips use — so the key can no longer drift from the thing it keys. Added the light-theme legend-pill background · verified in both themes at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the Ampèrian loop was the one glyph with no key entry** (a grey dashed ellipse sitting under a pink one). Added a dashed legend swatch, and updated the Controls Guide's colour-key step, which named only three of the four
- FIXED [P2][PHYS] **the plate charges were drawn on the OUTER faces** (`plateAX−10`, `plateBX+10`) while the code comment said "inner side" and the field is drawn between the plates. Moved to the facing surfaces, where the charge on a parallel-plate capacitor actually resides
- FIXED [P2][PHYS/D8] **at low current the scene showed an E field with no charge on the plates at all.** `floor(chargeVis·7)` is 0 for every `chargeVis < 1/7`, while the arrows are drawn from `chargeVis > 0.02` — so the whole early ramp (and the entire run at I = 0.05 A under the old freeze) pictured a field with no source. Charge count is now tied to the same threshold and floors at one pair
- FIXED [P2][D8] canvas labels below the 12px legibility floor at 1024×768 (the gap-rate line and the plate label rendered at 10.45px) — all ten `ctx.font` floors raised to 12px, and the rate line shortened so it still fits the narrow canvas · verified: smallest drawn glyph 12.0px at dpr 1, none below
- FIXED [P2][D8] the E arrows' alpha floor raised 0.40 → 0.55: at the bottom of the current range they blended to ~2:1 against the dark canvas and read as absent
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — draw the magnetic field on the Ampèrian loop, inside the gap as well as round the wire.** Ampère's law is a statement ABOUT B, and B is the one quantity this sim never shows: the loop is drawn, both surfaces are drawn, both tallies are drawn, and the student never sees the thing the enclosed current is supposed to determine. Without it "the two surfaces disagree" is an accounting puzzle rather than a physical contradiction, and the payoff — B is CONTINUOUS across the gap, B = μ₀I/(2πr) outside the plates and μ₀Ir/(2πR²) inside them — is invisible. Sketch: a second loop draggable along the axis (or a fixed pair, one round the wire and one between the plates) with tangential B arrows whose length is μ₀I_enc/(2πr) from the surface tally, a small B(r) readout in teslas, and the arrows going visibly DEAD in the gap when Maxwell's term is off and springing to the same length as at the wire when it is on. That single before/after is the whole lesson and it is currently carried by two numbers in a sidebar. (2) **a live Φ_E(t) / I(t) strip under the scene** — the causal chain Q↑ → E↑ → Φ_E↑ → dΦ_E/dt → I_d is currently asserted in text and a static number; a ramp whose SLOPE is read off as I_d, tracking a flat I_conduction line, makes "the rate equals the current" watchable instead of stated (this is the D2.2 causal-visibility gap, and the reason C scores 3). (3) **a with/without split view** — the Maxwell toggle is a mode switch, so the contrast that carries the concept is held in memory; a ghost of the other state (greyed bag tally and greyed B arrows) makes it simultaneous, which is what the topic's ratio-style JEE questions need. (4) **unlock plate area and separation.** Card 5 asserts "the identity I_d = I holds independently of the plate area or separation" and the sim's own audit invariant proves it, but both are hard-wired and invisible to the student, so the claim is checkable only from the Formal panel's algebra, never by experiment — two sliders would turn an assertion into a demonstration. (5) **an explicit "what would you have to believe" beat** — a second gate after the reveal, asking what physically crosses the gap, would put the sim's named target misconception in front of a commitment instead of in card 5's prose
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the `.legend` pill's 11px item text is shared shell CSS and was left alone in one file — mitigated instead by naming every glyph IN the scene (`flat disc`, `bulging bag`, `capacitor plates`, `electric field E`, `Ampèrian loop`), so the legend is no longer the only decoder. The `qVis` ceiling is a display cap and is now disclosed on the canvas. The E-field growth restarting when a slider moves is the shell's `restartOnParamChange` contract (a new current = a new run) and is correct here. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (every site resolves `--font-sans` through `getComputedStyle` and measures a real px size — smallest 12.0px); canvas theme read from `documentElement` while the class lands on `body` (this sim already read `document.body`, and the new palette does too); auto-scale cancelling a control (no auto-fit anywhere — the layout is a fixed fraction of the canvas and the I slider moves the tallies, the banner, the label and the growth rate); affine/clamped encodings (the one clamp, the arrow-length ceiling, is disclosed; the tallies are exact); temporal aliasing (nothing oscillates); Speed changing the answer (4× leaves all readouts identical). The only console entry in either theme at either viewport is the harness's `/favicon.ico` 404
- Gates: syntax ✓ (8/8 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ with `--baseline` against `git show 715b785:…` (welcome-overlay, guided-inquiry 5 cards, controls-tutorial 10 steps; 0 baseline errors ignored) · user-version-eval ✓ (exit 0) · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (repo `canvas-check.mjs` enumerates a hardcoded QM sim list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly against the pre-fix build: `#scene` 1.000/1.000, drift 0.0000) · copyright ✓ · `__audit` ratchet unchanged (both invariants pass: I_d − I = 1.11e−16, geometry spread = 0)
