## 2026-09-11 — Module-13-Optics/C204-total-internal-reflection-and-applications.html (jee-sim-deep-review, Track P + Track D incl. D8, Track L)
- Grid: 288 `__audit.at` points + 264 live-DOM points (n₁ ∈ {1.00,1.33,1.50,2.00,2.42,2.50} × n₂ ∈ {1.00,1.33,1.50,2.50} × θ₁ ∈ {0, 0.5, 20, 30, 41.5, 41.8, 42, 45, 48.8, 60, 89}°, including θ_c for glass/water/diamond, the n₁=n₂ degenerate case and the reversed n₁<n₂ branch) against an independent Python oracle (Snell, sin θ_c = n₂/n₁, Fresnel R/T for unpolarised light) — **0 mismatches**, before and after the fixes. Plus a new 42-point drawn-brightness grid (7 media pairs × 6 angles) reading the ray pixels off the canvas and comparing to the Fresnel oracle: worst |Δα| = 0.020. Ray geometry verified by ring-scanning the canvas at 10 (n₁,n₂,θ₁) points — drawn θ₁ and θ₂ match the oracle to ±0.1°. browser-verified: yes (Track P, D, D8 and L).
- Report card: V 4 · C 4 · R 4 · L 3 · G 4 · N 4  (pre-fix: V 3 · C 3 · R 2 · L 3 · G 2 · N 3)
- FIXED [P0][DEMO/PHYS] **the light's energy split was drawn as a constant, so the approach to θ_c was a cliff, not a fade.** The refracted ray was inked at full opacity and the reflected ray at a fixed `globalAlpha = 0.55` for every sub-critical angle, then the two swapped at θ_c. Measured: drawn brightness flat at 228 (refracted) / 133 (reflected) from θ₁ = 10° to 41°, while the Fresnel oracle has R climbing 0.042 → 0.380 and T falling 0.958 → 0.620 over that same span. A student was taught (a) that 55 % of the light reflects at normal incidence, where it is 4 %, and (b) that a full-strength refracted ray is switched off at a threshold — the exact misconception the Info modal names as its target. Both rays (and their arcs, arrowheads and photon dots) are now inked by the Fresnel share for unpolarised light, R = (r_s² + r_p²)/2 · verified: drawn alpha now tracks the oracle across 42 grid points to |Δα| ≤ 0.020; at 41.5° the scene reads "Refracted 46% / Reflected 54%" against an oracle T = 0.458, R = 0.542
- FIXED [P0][LAYER] **card 4's prediction was answered by the screen before it was asked.** `onStep(3)` pushed θ₁ to θ_c + 8°, so arriving at "Predict — go past θ_c. What does the refracted ray do?" the canvas already carried the TOTAL INTERNAL REFLECTION banner, the θ₂ readout already said "— (TIR)", the regime already said "TIR (θ₁ > θ_c)" and no refracted ray was drawn. The scene now holds at θ_c (θ₂ = 90.00°, no banner, grazing ray still present) and advances past θ_c only on the commit click · verified: at step 3 pre-commit the banner border pixel is background [13,34,34] and the regime reads "at θ_c"; post-commit it is #fbbf24 [237,181,35] and θ₁ = 49.8°
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **card 2's gate leaked θ_c too** — its correct option is the only one naming a critical angle, and both the sidebar readout (41.81°) and a labelled dashed reference ray were on screen before the student committed. θ_c is now staged: withheld through cards 1–2, revealed on commit or on reaching card 3, and always visible in Controls-Guide and Free modes · verified: `r-thetac` reads "—" at steps 0–1, "41.81°" from step 2, and "41.81°" throughout the controls-guide walk
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/FUNC] **the critical angle was not reachable and the slider desynced from the scene.** θ₁'s step was 0.5°, so no glass (41.81°), water (48.75°) or diamond (24.44°) critical angle sat on a step — and `onStep`'s `sTheta.value = tc.toFixed(1)` was silently snapped by the browser (41.8 → 42.0, i.e. straight into TIR), leaving the thumb, the "41.8°" echo and the drawn ray on three different angles; nudging any other slider then re-applied the snapped 42.0 through the shell's restart-on-param-change and flipped card 3's scene into TIR. step → 0.1° · verified: step 2 now reports slider "41.8" / echo "41.8°" / θ₂ "90.00°", and the whole grid runs with zero step-snap events
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/D8] **the canvas stayed dark in the light theme, with near-black text on it.** `css()` read the custom properties off `document.documentElement` while the light theme lands on `body.light-theme`, so `--bg` always resolved to #060E11; meanwhile `isLight` (read correctly off body) switched the drawn labels to #0f172a. Measured: page background rgb(226,232,240), canvas corner [13,21,25]. `css()` now reads `document.body`, and the `.legend` pill (hardcoded `rgba(6,14,17,.72)`, i.e. dark chrome behind dark text) gets a light-theme background · verified: canvas corner is now [222,228,236] in light and [10,18,22] in dark
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the three rays were named only in an 11px pill 885 px away from where they are drawn, and colour was the only channel telling them apart.** Each ray now carries its name in the scene at its own far end — "Incident", "Refracted 46%", "Reflected 54%" — drawn at full opacity even when its ray is a 4 % ghost, with a darker same-hue ink in the light theme. The percentages double as the legend for the new brightness encoding, which the plot title glosses once ("each ray's brightness = its share of the light") · verified at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- FIXED [P1][D8] **two unexplained glyphs.** The dashed vertical every card measures angles from was unlabelled — it now reads "normal" (clamped below the plot title, which the 550px-tall canvas at 1024×768 reaches). The faint dashed amber reference ray read a bare "θ_c=41.8°"; it now reads "critical angle θ_c=41.8°" · verified at both viewports
- FIXED [P2][D8] **angle labels were painted over the lines they annotate** — at 30° "θ₁=30.0°" sat across the dashed normal, and "θ₁" and "θ_r" collided with each other in TIR. All four canvas angle labels now draw on a theme-coloured background chip, and the θ₁ label moved from r = arcR+16 to arcR+34, clear of θ_r's radius · verified at 1500×950 and 1024×768
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the "denser → rarer" half of the TIR condition was never stated in the picture** — the regime readout said only "rare→dense", leaving the half students actually drop as an inference. It now reads "rare→dense (no TIR)", and card 5 points at it and at the 89° sweep that shows the refracted ray never leaving
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] **the welcome overlay gave away card 4.** Blurb and voiceover line 2 both read "Above the critical angle, no refracted ray exists — all the light reflects", which is card 4's correct option in different words, delivered before the student picks a mode. Reframed to "A ray leaves glass for air. Tilt it far enough and the boundary stops behaving like a window." The hero animation (equal-Δθ₁ dots of the refracted ray) is left as-is: it is abstract enough not to be decodable as the answer, and redrawing it would be a representation change
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] card 3 instructed "Set θ₁ to the critical angle", an action `onStep(2)` already performs. Rewritten as an observation of what is on screen (θ₂ = 90.00°, the transmitted share fallen to zero), per the SME grammar's no-UI-choreography rule; card 5 updated to name the new regime readout
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a θ₂-vs-θ₁ curve with a cursor locked to the ray, and its vertical asymptote at θ_c.** The mechanism of this topic is "sin θ₂ = (n₁/n₂)·sin θ₁ with n₁/n₂ > 1, so sin θ₂ reaches its ceiling of 1 at a finite θ₁" — a statement about a function that is nowhere plotted. Sketch: a ~200×140 inset plotting θ₂ against θ₁ over 0–90°, the curve rising and turning vertical at θ_c with the region beyond shaded "no solution", a dot at the live θ₁ that runs up the curve as the slider moves, and the curve redrawn (θ_c sliding) when n₁ or n₂ changes; flipping to n₁ < n₂ shows a curve that never reaches 90°, which is the "denser to rarer" condition made visible instead of asserted. (2) **the applications are missing from a sim whose title is "and applications"** — fibre, prism and diamond exist only as sentences in card 6 and the Info modal. Sketch: a preset row (Optical fibre / 45-45-90 prism / Diamond) that swaps the flat interface for the corresponding geometry and traces the ray through several bounces, with the θ_c readout and the TIR test reused unchanged at each surface; the fibre case adds the acceptance cone sin θ_a = √(n₁²−n₂²)/n₀, which is directly examinable. (3) **an R/T energy bar pair**, so the split the rays now encode as brightness is also readable as a number-free quantity and the "energy is conserved, not created" point lands — a two-segment 100 % bar under the scene, animated as θ₁ sweeps. (4) **a ghost of the sub-critical ray retained when the student crosses θ_c**, so the before/after contrast is on screen simultaneously rather than held in memory. (5) **live substitution in the Formal panel** — render sin θ_c = n₂/n₁ with the current numbers filled in and the arithmetic shown, binding the equation to the drawing (Ainsworth linkage); the panel currently shows only symbols and is collapsed by default. (6) **a Brewster-angle marker on the incident side** — θ_B = arctan(n₂/n₁) is where the new Fresnel encoding already produces a visible dip, and it is now free to label.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the `.legend`, `.plot-title`, `.aside-zone-head h3` and `.shell-panel-head h3` chrome sits at 11px, below the D8 13px bar, but is platform-shared template CSS rather than this file's — and every ray is now named in the scene at 12px, so the 11px legend is no longer load-bearing. The Readouts panel falls below the fold at 1024×768 in guided-inquiry mode (the sidebar scrolls to it), mitigated by θ₁, θ₂ and θ_c all being drawn in the scene. `onStep(2)` sets θ₁ to the exact θ_c while the slider shows 41.8°, a 0.01° residual that keeps the θ₂ = 90.00° money shot. At θ₁ = 0 the incident and reflected rays are the same line by construction; their tags are offset to different rows. Named defect classes checked explicitly: `ctx.font` with `var()` — 0 occurrences, all 7 sites literal px; theme read from `documentElement` — found and fixed; pause-desync — not present (`cfg.gateLocksPlay` is false, so gates never pause the loop, and the build is `no-transport` with a free-running rAF); Speed changing the answer — Speed is hidden by `no-transport` and only scales the photon phase; auto-scale cancelling a control — the ray length is a fixed fraction of the canvas, not fitted to θ; predict-gate leaks including the welcome hero — found at both gates and at the welcome blurb, all three fixed; temporal aliasing — the photon phase advances 0.6·dt per second, ~0.01 per frame. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (7/7 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, controls-tutorial 9 steps, guided-inquiry 6 cards) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check — the repo script enumerates a hardcoded QM sim list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly against the pre-fix file at both viewports: `#scene` 1150×771/1150×771 and 674×550/674×550, ratio 1.000, identical pre→post ✓ · copyright ✓
- S3: re-push required for this file.

## C185 motional-emf — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-12-EMI-AC-and-EM-Waves/C185-motional-emf.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (D1 blind spec → Track P → Track D incl. D8 → Track L → fixes + gates)
- TRACK P: **22 live grid points × 5 quantities = 110 comparisons, 0 mismatches** — v ∈ {0, 0.1, 1.5, 3, 4, 8} m/s × B ∈ {0.1, 0.5, 0.8, 1.5} T × R ∈ {0.5, 2, 5, 10} Ω swept one-at-a-time from the defaults plus 8 cross-combinations of extremes, read from the real sliders through the DOM readouts against an independent first-principles oracle (ε = BLv, I = ε/R, F = BIL = B²L²v/R, P = F·v). **The JEE-critical chain checks out end to end**: the retarding force is anti-parallel to v by construction (drawn left for the only direction of motion the sim offers), the drawn current sense is clockwise — top rail resistor→rod, down the rod, bottom rail rod→resistor — which puts the induced field INTO the page against a rising out-of-page flux (Lenz) and makes I L × B point in −x̂, and the power balance closes exactly: `__audit.at()` agrees with the oracle to 1.5e-16 relative at all three corners with I²R ≡ F·v to the last bit (11.520000 vs 11.520000 at B=1.5, v=8, R=0.5). `__audit.run()` pass (worst |P_mech − P_elec| = 5.6e-17 vs tol 1e-9) · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:4 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:2 R:2 L:3 G:3 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (5 cards walked from the welcome overlay, both gates answered; Controls Guide walks all 10 steps; `__cgReveals`/`__freeExplore`/`__giResume` all lift the staged canvas ledger and the staged formula labels; Reset restores v/B/R, rod position, step 0 and the plain-name labels)
- **THE AUTO-SCALE CLASS WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED — AND IT WAS DEAD AT THE DEFAULTS.** `drawLedger` scaled to `Pmax = max(0.05, 1.4·max(pMech,pElec))`, i.e. to the value it was drawing, so for any P above 0.036 W the fill was exactly 1/1.4 = 71.4 % of the track. The default state is 0.045 W, already past that: sampling the bar row gave **713.0 px at v = 3, 4, 6 and 8** (P 0.045 → 0.32 W, ×7.1) and **713.0 px at B = 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5** (P ×9) — pixel-identical every time, while card 4 sends the student to this exact panel to double v and double B and watch it. The old code comment's "worst-case P ≈ 0.28" was wrong by 41× (the slider box reaches 11.52 W). Fixed below
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the power ledger was dead by auto-scale at the default state** (evidence above). Replaced with a FIXED 0.40 W full scale — chosen to cover every single-slider sweep from the defaults — printed on the panel, with the bars marked "bars clipped" beyond it instead of silently rescaling · verified: bar extent 22 / 92 / 370 / 822 px against predicted 23 / 93 / 371 / 824 at P = 0.011 / 0.045 / 0.180 / 11.52 W, doubling v gives **×4.02 and ×4.18 in pixels against physics ×4.00**, doubling B **×4.02**, and both bars stay identical at every grid point (0 px at v = 0)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the retarding-force arrow saturated at a flat `Math.min(180, …)`, falsifying card 3's own resolved answer.** Measured px/F constant at 1980 up to F = 0.09 N then collapsing: 179 px at B = 1.25, 179 px at B = 1.4, 179 px at B = 1.5 while F ran 0.094 → 0.135 N, and still 179 px at the slider-box maximum F = 1.44 N — a 16× understatement. Doubling B from 0.75 → 1.5 T lengthened the arrow **×2.55, not the ×4 card 3 resolves to and card 4 instructs**. The gain is now a single `state.scale · 2.4` px/N (exactly the old 2000 px/N at the 1150-wide default layout, and viewport-independent), the only cap is the live geometric room to the resistor branch, and when that bites the tail carries a double-slash break and the label says "(arrow clipped)" · verified: drawn == wanted at **every** point of the B slider at both viewports (270/270 px at 1500×950, 141/141 px at 1024×768), doublings 0.5→1.0 = ×4.14 / ×4.13 and 0.75→1.5 = ×4.03 / ×4.15
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the velocity arrow was affine, not proportional** — `20 + 60·(v/V_MAX)`, a 20 px pedestal on the one arrow whose parameter card 2 is about. Measured px/v fell from 48.0 at v = 0.5 to 10.0 at v = 8, so doubling v from 2 → 4 m/s lengthened it ×1.43 (35 → 50 px). Now `80·(v/V_MAX)` · verified: **px/v = 10.0 flat from v = 1 to v = 8**, same length at V_MAX as before, and the arrow vanishes at v = 0
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the induced current — the middle of the causal chain — was two 14 px half-arrows drawn exactly ON the rails.** `drawCurrentArrows` stroked 2 px of `#3b9b8f` at `yT`/`yB`, the same pixels as the 3 px `#cbd5e1` rails, with no arrow in the rod and none in the resistor branch: the student asked to reason I = ε/R → F = BIL could not see which way I went or that it was a closed loop. The two rail arrows now sit 9 px INSIDE the loop on dark background at ±20 px, and the two missing legs are drawn — down the rod (the leg I L × B acts on) and up through the resistor — so the circulation is traceable. Label reworded "I = …" → "induced current I = …" · verified in screenshots at both viewports, both themes
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak: the Live-readouts labels spelled out the whole derivation at both gated cards.** At card 2 ("double v → F?") and card 3 ("double B → F?") the sidebar showed, from load, "EMF ε = BLv" / "Current I = ε/R" / "Retarding F = BIL" — chain those three and both answers fall out (linear in v; B enters twice, so ×4) — and the panel's own note repeated "ε = BLv". Labels now carry the plain name until step 4 and the formula from step 4 (where the sim wants them used), via a `show-formulas` class driven by the existing `inquiryStep()`; the note keeps the disclosure and drops the formula; the canvas force label reads "retarding force F = …" before step 4. Free exploration and the Controls Guide set `revealAs`, so both get the formulas immediately · verified live: plain names at cards 1–3, formulas at cards 4–5, plain again after Reset
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the legend was dark-on-dark in light theme** — `.legend` hard-codes `background:rgba(6,14,17,.72)` while its text follows `--text`, which flips to `rgb(15,23,42)`: computed contrast **2.18:1** against the composited `rgb(74,80,83)` fill. The scene's only key was unreadable for every light-theme user · verified: 2.18 → **17.06:1** with a `rgba(248,250,252,.86)` light-theme fill, dark theme unchanged at 15.79:1
- FIXED [P2][D8] **the ⊙ glyph the code promised was never drawn, and its gloss was 11 px in a far corner.** `drawFieldLegend` carried the comment "small ⊙ symbol next to it" with no glyph, at 11 px, at `viewH*0.14` — 250 px from the dots it explains, and at 1024×768 directly under the legend pill. The ring-and-dot glyph is now drawn in place, the text is 12 px and reads "= magnetic field B = … T, pointing OUT of the page (uniform, constant)", and the whole line moved under the bottom rail, right against the dots it glosses. The ledger title went 11 → 12 px · verified at both viewports, both themes
- FIXED [P2][D8] **the legend listed 3 of the 5 drawn encodings and gave one colour two meanings.** Velocity (cyan) and induced current (teal) — the two arrows carrying the causal chain — were absent, and `#3b9b8f` was simultaneously the swept-area chip and the current arrows. Added "→ velocity v" and "⟳ induced current I" chips, gave the current chip a square marker and every chip a shape prefix so colour is never the only channel
- FIXED [P2][D8] **three label-placement rules were keyed to the canvas edge instead of to the occupied geometry**, which the honest-length fixes then exposed: the roaming force label's "room on the left" test was `>= 4` and laid it straight across the resistor's zig-zag at 1024×768/B = 1.5 (now `>= xPx(0) + 4`); the ledger's two header strings both anchored to the track and ran through each other once the track shortened (the full-scale note now anchors to the canvas lane); and `drawBar` dropped its label INSIDE the track, harmless while the fill was pinned at 71 % but half light-on-orange once the fill became honest (the track now reserves a 236 px label lane). Rod start moved 0.20 → 0.40 m so the full B sweep has room to be drawn at all · verified: no overlapping pairs at 1500×950 or 1024×768, at default / B-max / v-max-R-min / all-max / v = 0, both themes
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] card 4's two central claims were the ones the pixels falsified ("the arrow lengthens ×2 / ×4", "the two bars are always equal" — true only because they never moved). Rewritten to name what now actually happens, including the bars' ×4 and the 0.40 W scale, and the UI-choreography opener ("Now grab the sliders") dropped per the SME grammar
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — draw the applied pull force.** The sim is titled "electricity costs you a push" and the push is the one thing never drawn: the rod moves at constant v with a single leftward arrow on it, a free-body diagram that contradicts Newton's first law as presented, and the ledger's "Mech. in F·v" silently means the *applied* force while the only F on screen is the retarding one. Sketch: a second arrow at the rod, same length as the retarding arrow and exactly opposite, in the "you" amber, labelled "your pull F_app = … N", a legend chip, the top bar relabelled "Mech. in F_app·v", and a sentence in card 5 — which turns P_mech = εI from an asserted identity into a drawn one and kills the "the rod just glides" misconception card 5 exists to attack. (2) **reverse the motion (v < 0).** The Lenz punchline is "the force ALWAYS opposes v" and only half of it is shown: the slider is [0, 8] and the rod only ever goes right, so a student never sees the current and the force both flip while the opposition survives. Needs the swept-area sign test, the rod wrap and the three arrow senses driven off sign(v). (3) **a "released" mode — the exponential decay.** m dv/dt = −B²L²v/R, v = v₀e^{−t/τ} with τ = mR/(B²L²), total heat = ½mv₀², and q = BLΔx/R independent of time, are JEE staples and are all unreachable because v is a slider the sim never integrates. Sketch: a Driven/Released toggle plus a mass slider, semi-implicit stepping in Released, the v readout driven from state, and a v(t) trace. (4) **an open-circuit contrast** — a switch in the left branch: ε stays BLv, I and F go to zero, the rod coasts. That EMF exists without current is the standard discriminator and the sim currently cannot show it. (5) **a live ε(t) or v(t) graph** cursor-synced to the animation, the one representation from the D1 spec the sim has none of
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the retarding arrow's 7 px arrowhead sets a floor, so below F ≈ 4 mN (B < 0.25 T at defaults, where the readout shows 0.0006 N) the drawn mark is head-sized rather than scale-length — a minimum mark size, not a scale lie, and the numeric readout is exact there. The on-screen motion is slowed by `PLAYBACK_SCALE = 0.08` and the rail gap is not to scale against the 1.2 m track; both are disclosed in the sidebar note, which the fix rewrote but kept. The rod recycles to its start at the end of each sweep (steady-state phenomenon, commented). Shell template chrome below the D8 13px bar (`.control-range` 12px, panel `h3` 11px, Listen 9px, Auto 10px) is platform-shared CSS and was not changed in one file. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (all 4 distinct font sites are literal px — `css('--font-sans')` returns a resolved family list, not a `var()` token), theme read from `documentElement` while the class lands on `body` (all 8 read sites use `document.body.classList`, which is exactly where the toggle puts it, and the toggle refits), pause-desync (every slider handler calls `draw()` directly — canvas hash changes on a slider move while paused and is stable between moves), Speed changing the answer (readouts depend only on the sliders; `dt` is clamped at both ends so a backgrounded tab cannot blow the state up), temporal aliasing (the rod advances at most 36 px/frame at v = 8 × 4× speed on a 1000 px track, with no periodic structure to beat against), welcome-hero gate leak (the hero is an abstract fringe motif with its caption `display:none` and shows no force, current or power). The only console entry in either theme at either viewport is the harness's `/favicon.ico` 404
- Gates: syntax ✓ (8/8 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (guided-inquiry 5 cards, controls-tutorial 10 steps, welcome-overlay) · user-version-eval ✓ (exit 0) · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (repo `canvas-check.mjs` enumerates a hardcoded QM sim list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly against the pre-fix build: `#scene` 1150×771 / 1150×771 at 1500×950 and 674×550 / 674×550 at 1024×768, ratio 1.000/1.000, drift 0.0000 pre→post, and re-confirmed after the `.legend` CSS change) · copyright ✓ · `__audit` ratchet unchanged (`mechanicalPowerEqualsElectricalPower` pass, 5.6e-17 vs tol 1e-9)
- S3: re-push required for this file.

## C199 electromagnetic-waves-nature-and-properties — deep review 2026-09-11

- FILE: `Module-12-EMI-AC-and-EM-Waves/C199-electromagnetic-waves-nature-and-properties.html` (jee-sim-deep-review: D1 blind spec first, then Track P, Track D incl. D8 at 1500×950 and 1024×768, Track L)
- TRACK P: grid 25 (f × E₀ combinations incl. both slider extremes, every band boundary, and every physically special point) + 16-point independent Python oracle · 0 mismatches · browser-verified yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:3 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:2 C:2 R:1 L:2 G:2 N:2)
- FIXED [P0][PHYS/DEMO] **E and B were drawn parallel, in the same screen plane.** Both fields were plotted as vertical arrows off one axis, 4 px apart along z, while the plot title claimed "E in x-plane, B in y-plane" and the Info modal claimed "E ⊥ B ⊥ k" — the single most important structural fact of the topic was contradicted by the pixels. B is now swept along an oblique screen direction (the y-axis, pointing out of the screen), the two fields are backed by translucent planes so the pair reads as three dimensions, and a labelled E/B/z triad names the three directions. Handedness checked: E up (x̂) × B out-of-screen (ŷ) = travel right (ẑ), matching the drawn E × B arrow · verified: pixel phase-lag scan gives −17° at 1500×950 and −28° at 1024×768 (residual = the documented projection shear), never the ±90° the classic error would produce; corr 0.91–0.95
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/PHYS] **catastrophic float cancellation made the wave a staircase and the animation pseudo-random.** `fieldsAt` was evaluated at the TRUE ω (≈6×10¹⁴ rad/s) with `t` in real seconds, so within a few seconds ω·t ≈ 10¹⁵ and double precision resolved the phase only to ~0.25 rad: the sine rendered as a visible staircase (screenshotted pre-fix) and the phase jumped by ~10¹³ rad per frame, i.e. randomly, so nothing ever appeared to travel. A `phaseTime(f) = DISPLAY_HZ·t/f` conversion now feeds the same engine op an argument of order 10⁻¹⁵ s, giving ω·t of order 1 · verified: E-curve y at a fixed column now advances smoothly (443→426→398→367→341→313→292→282→280→289 over 10 samples) instead of jumping; the slowdown is disclosed on the sidebar note
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the E₀ slider moved no pixels** — the classic auto-scale kill. The drawn height was `E/state.E0 × plotHalf`, i.e. normalised by the control's own value, so 1 V/m and 40 V/m rendered pixel-identically (measured: eSpan 515/515/515/520/520 px across the whole range) while card 4 told the student "the blue arrows grow — and so do the pink ones, in lockstep". Now referred to a fixed full scale `E0_FULL = 40` · verified: peak |E| = 5/22/44/86/129/171 px at E₀ = 1/5/10/20/30/40, i.e. 4.28–4.40 px per V/m — linear; peak |B| / peak |E| = 0.544 at every setting, so the lockstep card 4 claims is now literally true on screen
- FIXED [P1][FUNC] **the canvas never re-read the theme.** `css()` read the custom properties off `document.documentElement`, but the light theme lands as `body.light-theme`, so the plot stayed near-black on a light page (measured bg pixel `[6,14,17]` in light theme) and took the DOM plot title and the legend text down with it — dark text on a dark canvas. `css()` now reads `document.body`; E/B/E×B get theme-specific inks (amber → `#b45309` in light, which is legible on white); the `.legend` pill and its dots get light-theme overrides · verified: bg pixel `[238,240,242]` in light theme, screenshotted at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][FUNC] **ghost trails.** `--surface` is `rgba(…,.95)`, so filling it straight over the previous frame left 5 % of it behind every time; at small E₀ the accumulated silhouettes of earlier, taller waves were plainly visible. `clearRect` before the fill
- FIXED [P1][FUNC] **pause-desync.** The slider handlers carried the comment "no need to force draw — animation loop is running", but the loop only draws while playing: a student who paused to line up a value dragged against a frozen canvas. Both handlers now repaint · verified: pixel hash changes on E₀ and on f while `Shell.playing === false`
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **card 3's answer was printed on the canvas from card 1.** The permanent caption "E and B in phase — peak together, cross zero together" sat above the wave while card 3 asked the student to decide whether the fields take turns. An `onStep` hook now shows a neutral naming caption ("Blue arrows: the electric field E. Pink arrows: the magnetic field B.") until card 3, and the full claim from card 3 onward; the Controls Guide, free exploration and lecture mode always get the full caption · verified: screenshotted at card 1 and at card 3
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the slider promised γ and stopped short of it.** Range ended at 10¹⁹ Hz while its own end-label read "10¹⁹ Hz (γ)"; at that setting `bandName` returns X-ray (γ starts at 3×10¹⁹), the spectrum strip's γ band was entirely off-axis, and the sidebar's "changes by 14 decades" was false over a 13-decade slider (title: "fourteen decades"). Range extended to 10²⁰ Hz, strip axis to 10²⁰, label to "10²⁰ Hz (γ-ray)", card 1 and the Info modal reworded from "X-ray / twelve orders" to "γ-ray / fourteen orders", audit invariant loop widened · verified: at fexp 19.48 and 20 the band readout is "γ-ray" and the γ band is on the strip
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **card 1 and card 4 asked about quantities the sim never showed.** Card 1's whole prediction is about B₀ and card 4 claims "doubling E₀ quadruples I", but the only readouts were λ and the E₀/B₀ ratio. Added `B₀` and `I` rows to the existing Live-readouts box · verified against the oracle at every grid point: B₀ = 6.67×10⁻⁸ T at E₀ = 20 (oracle 6.671×10⁻⁸), I = 5.31×10⁻¹ W/m² (oracle 5.308×10⁻¹), I = 1.33×10⁻³ at E₀ = 1 — the ×1600 that card 4 needs
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **c = 1/√(ε₀μ₀) was absent** — the step that identified light as an electromagnetic wave, and the most-examined single fact in this chapter, appeared nowhere. Added as a fifth Formal row, with the arithmetic done live through `Engine.emwave.lightSpeedFromConstants()` rather than asserted · verified: renders `2.99792 × 10⁸ m/s`, oracle `1/√(ε₀μ₀) = 299792458.0000065`, relative error 2.2×10⁻¹⁴
- FIXED [P1][D8] **overlapping canvas text at 1024×768.** The frequency-marker label, the "B arrows scaled ×c" note and the z-axis title printed on top of each other, and the E × B arrow was drawn straight through the waveform. The marker label moved below the tick row, the travel arrow to the clear band under the legend, the two scale disclosures into the sidebar note (where they get full-size DOM text), and a reserved `keyBand` was added to the layout so the triad cannot land on the spectrum strip when the viewport is short · verified: screenshotted clean at 1024×768 and 1500×950, both themes
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the one band a student can actually see was the only unlabelled one.** "visible" spans ~19 px of the strip against a hard-coded 34 px labelling threshold, so it rendered as a nameless green sliver. The threshold is now a measured text width and narrow bands get a leader-line callout · verified: "visible" and "γ" both labelled at both viewports
- FIXED [P2][D8] every canvas label raised to ≥12 px (was 10 / 10.5 / 11 px for the band names, decade ticks, B-scale note and in-phase caption); legend items reworded from bare symbols to words ("E — electric field (V/m)")
- FIXED [P2][FUNC] the Feedback pill and its modal tripped the shell's restart-on-parameter-change watcher (no `data-no-reset`, not shell-owned), so reporting a bug silently reset both sliders to defaults. `data-no-reset` added to the pill and the backdrop
- FIXED [P2][PHYS] `fmtSci` mapped the exponent's minus to U+2212 and then superscripted only the digits, printing `10−⁸` instead of `10⁻⁸` — visible in the new B₀ and I readouts
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] default E₀ moved 10 → 20 V/m (mid-slider) so the honest linear encoding still fills half the frame at boot and the student can explore in both directions; card 1's feedback number updated to 6.67×10⁻⁸ T
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a log-scaled wavelength ruler with real-world size anchors.** The view auto-fits 2.6 λ at every frequency, so the drawn wave is pixel-identical from radio to γ (measured: λ = 400.0 px at fexp 6, 10, 14, 17 and 20) and the thesis of the sim — "wavelength collapses by fourteen decades" — is currently only a number and a disclosure. Sketch: a 60 px strip under the spectrum bar carrying a log-λ axis from 10³ m to 10⁻¹² m with a marker tracking the slider and fixed reference ticks (football field / hand / grain of sand / virus / atom / nucleus); ~40 lines, reuses `xOf`. (2) **a "true scale" toggle for B** that animates the pink arrows collapsing to their real 1/c height and back, turning the ×3×10⁸ disclosure into something the student sees rather than reads. (3) **radiation pressure**: a small absorbing/reflecting target fed by `Engine.emwave.radiationPressure(I)` showing p = I/c vs 2I/c — the factor of two is a standard JEE question and the engine op is already loaded. (4) **an in-medium mode** (`Engine.emwave.inMedium`) so the f-fixed / λ-shortens invariant is demonstrable. (5) **photon energy in eV** from `Engine.emwave.photon({frequency})`, tying the spectrum strip to the quantum chapter.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): at E₀ = 1 V/m the wave is a ~4 px ripple — that is the honest linear consequence of a 40× amplitude range, and the readouts carry the numbers; the λ-in-pixels invariance is inherent (fourteen decades cannot share one linear window) and is now disclosed in the card, the sidebar note and the per-λ axis label; the welcome hero draws its B dots exactly under its E dots (B·c/E₀ ≡ E/E₀) so the pink row is occluded — left alone deliberately, since making it visible would leak card 3's answer on the landing screen, and the honest fix is enhancement (1). Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (all 11 sites are literal px), unbounded KaTeX retry (bounded by the `typeof katex` guard and 60 ms backoff), view-only buttons missing `data-no-reset` (both sliders carry it; the pill now does too), silent canvas clamps at slider extremes, affine/clamped encodings. Console is clean at both viewports in both themes apart from the harness's `/favicon.ico` 404
- Gates: syntax ✓ (8/8 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay; controls-tutorial 10 steps; guided-inquiry 4 cards — both card-copy budgets tripped on the first rewrite and were re-trimmed) · user-version-eval ✓ (`all 1 builds pass`) · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (the repo script enumerates a hardcoded QM directory and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display ratio was measured directly: 1.000/1.000 at 1500×950 and at 1024×768, stable across a live resize) · copyright ✓
- browser-verified: Track P yes · Track D yes · Track D8 yes (1500×950 and 1024×768, dark and light) · Track L yes (welcome → all three modes, 4-card walk with both gate states, Finish, Reset, Maximize, Formal, Info, Controls Guide 10 steps, theme toggle, speed 1×/2×/4×)
- S3: re-push required for this file.

## C216 resolving-power-of-instruments — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-13-Optics/C216-resolving-power-of-instruments.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)
- TRACK P: **46 live grid points, 0 mismatches.** D ∈ {0.5, 1, 2, 3, 3.4, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 20} mm × λ ∈ {380, 400, 450, 550, 650, 700} nm × s ∈ {0.2, 1, 2, 2.7, 3.35, 3.4, 4, 8} mm swept one-at-a-time plus 6 cross-combinations of extremes, each read off the LIVE DOM (`#statTheta`, `#statSepMin`, `#statVerdict`) against an independent Python oracle that computes J₁ from its power series (not the sim's Abramowitz–Stegun rational approximation). θ_min = 1.22 λ/D and s_min = L·tan θ_min agree to displayed precision at every point (e.g. D = 0.5 mm/550 nm → 13.420 mm; D = 20 mm/700 nm → 0.427 mm; D = 3.4 mm/550 nm → 1.974 mm). The 1.22 is right and is circular-aperture-only — the oracle puts the first zero of J₁ at 3.831706, so 3.8317/π = 1.21967, and `Engine.wavefront.rayleighResolution` uses 1.00 for `shape:'slit'`. Sources are added as INTENSITIES (`airyIntensity(u1) + airyIntensity(u2)`), never amplitudes — there are no interference fringes anywhere. The verdict flips exactly at s = s_min on all three sliders (D: 3.3 → unresolved, 3.4 → resolved against a 3.355 mm threshold; s: 3.35 → unresolved, 3.40 → resolved). The drawn profile's valley floor now matches the oracle to the printed digit: 71% at s/s_min = 1.0132 (oracle 71%), 90% at 0.8942 (oracle 90%), 0–2% at 2.38 (oracle 2%). No NaN/Infinity/negative at any extreme; `timeless:true` so there is no integrator, no Speed control and nothing for a background tab to drift · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:3 R:4 L:3 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:2 C:2 R:2 L:3 G:2 N:2) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes, both device pixel ratios
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (welcome → all three modes; 4-card walk with both gate states; Reset → card 1, defaults, staging restored; Controls Guide 9 steps; theme toggle; Maximize)
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **on every HiDPI screen the entire Airy pattern was drawn in the top-left QUARTER of the scene canvas.** `drawScene` built `ctx.createImageData(w, h)` in CSS pixels and wrote it with `putImageData`, which ignores the DPR transform — so at devicePixelRatio 2 a 1150×464 bitmap landed in a 2300×929 backing store. Measured pre-fix at dpr 2: brightest pixel at (572, 228) against a canvas centre of (1150, 465), luminance 13 at the true centre and 255 at the quarter point, with a hard seam where the bitmap ended; the `star A`/`star B` ticks and the `s = …` caption — drawn through the scaled context — pointed at empty space 400 px away. Headless Chrome defaults to dpr 1, which is why it shipped. The bitmap is now blitted through an offscreen canvas with `ctx.drawImage(off, 0, 0, w, h)` so the DPR transform applies · verified: post-fix at dpr 2 the brightest pixel is at (1074–1152, 396–468) against centre (1150, 465), and every measured blob width is exactly 2× its dpr-1 value
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the auto-fit exactly cancelled the aperture and wavelength sliders — the mag-cyclotron defect class, verbatim.** The view extent was `max(α·0.8+4θ₁, θ₁·6, α·1.2+2θ₁)`; the middle term dominates for θ₁ > 0.4α, i.e. for D from 0.5 to 8.4 mm at the default separation and across the ENTIRE 380–700 nm range, so the drawn Airy radius was pinned at exactly 1/6 of the half-window. Measured pre-fix: the blob spanned 78 / 77 / 77 px at D = 0.5 / 1 / 2 mm (a 4× aperture change, pixel-identical) and 86 / 74 px at λ = 380 / 700 nm (a 1.84× change in θ_min, 12% of pixels, and in the *wrong* direction for the half-max span). Meanwhile the sim's own Info modal claimed "widening D visibly shrinks each disc" and card 2 said "watch the two Airy discs shrink" — a false dependence taught with correct equations. The window is now anchored to the SOURCE SEPARATION, `max(α·2.6, θ₁·1.3)`, so the pair holds still on screen and θ₁ alone sets the disc size; the θ₁ term only takes over once the disc exceeds 2α, deep in the unresolved regime where the picture is one blob at any scale · verified: post-fix the blob spans 269 / 292 / 292 / 214 / 197 / 159 / 107 / 70 / 42 px across D = 0.5 → 20 mm (a 7× shrink) and 219 / 249 / 292 / 333 / 331 px across λ = 380 → 700 nm; the same fix restores the s slider (262 / 292 / 197 / 107 px) which was also flat
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the profile asserted "✗ no dip" over a curve that plainly had one, and defined Rayleigh's criterion wrongly.** The label was driven by `resolvedFlag`, not by the drawn curve. A dip first appears at the SPARROW separation, which the oracle puts at 0.7767 × Rayleigh, so for every s/s_min in [0.78, 1.00) the sim printed "no dip" beside a visible valley — 10% deep at s/s_min = 0.894. The sidebar note ("the dip in the combined profile just becomes visible"), card 2 ("the Rayleigh moment is the instant the dip just becomes visible") and the Info modal ("the Rayleigh moment is marked where it just vanishes") all repeated the error. Rayleigh is a CONVENTION — peak-on-first-dark-ring, valley floor at 73.5% of the peak — not the moment a valley appears. The already-computed-but-unused `dipDepth` is now the live readout: the profile prints the measured valley floor as a percentage, draws the 73.5% Rayleigh line as a dashed reference with a dot on the actual valley floor, and all four copies of the false claim were rewritten · verified against the oracle at six separations (71% / 90% / 100% / 45% / 2% / 99% drawn vs 71 / 90 / 100 / 45 / 2 / 99 computed)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the tone map was applied per source and so was not a function of the total intensity.** `I₁^0.6·tint₁ + I₂^0.6·tint₂` means two pixels with the same combined intensity render differently depending on how the light splits between the stars, and it inflated the midpoint of a Rayleigh-separated pair from a true 0.735 to ≈0.85 of the peak — flattening the one observable the sim exists to show. The γ = 0.6 compression (which is needed, and right, to make the faint rings visible) is now applied ONCE to I₁+I₂ and the colour is the intensity-weighted mixture. The compression is also now disclosed on the canvas ("brightness on a compressed scale, so the faint rings show"), with the linear profile named as the place to read the valley — the skill's not-to-scale disclosure rule
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the welcome layer answered the first gated card three times over.** The blurb read "Two stars merge into one blob until the aperture is wide enough — magnification never splits them"; narration line 2 read "Widening the aperture shrinks each Airy disc. Magnifying the image just makes a bigger blob"; and the hero canvas plotted θ_min falling with D against a deliberately flat "magnification" line, with a hero caption saying so. Card 1's three choices are *magnify* / *widen the aperture* / *more light*. All three rewritten to pose the question, and the hero redrawn as the phenomenon alone — seven pairs of point sources drawn progressively closer until they merge, composited with `globalCompositeOperation:'lighter'` so even the hero adds brightnesses rather than amplitudes
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the sidebar and canvas leaked the same answer pre-commit.** At gated card 1 the Rayleigh readouts panel showed `θmin = 1.22 λ/D`, the canvas carried a `θ = 1.22 λ/D (Airy radius)` scale bar and the profile printed `s / s_min`, all naming D as the lever before the prediction. A `revealTheory` flag (its own `.stage-hidden` class — NOT `.cg-hidden`, which must be absent from the DOM at boot or the controls-tutorial verifier fails) stages the readouts panel, the scale bar, the 73.5% reference line and the ratio line at step 0 only. The apparatus, both stars, the separation caption and the RESOLVED/UNRESOLVED verdict stay — card 1's own text states "they look like one fuzzy blob", so the verdict is the premise, not the answer. Everything returns on the first `onStep(i≠0)`, and `__cgReveals` / `__freeExplore` lift it outright · verified: `display:none` at step 0, `block` after any choice is clicked, `block` throughout the Controls Guide and free exploration, and re-staged by ↻ Reset
- FIXED [P1][PHYS/LAYER] **card 1's feedback named the wrong threshold.** "at about 5 mm the two discs pull apart" — the sim's own verdict flips at D = 1.22λL/s = 3.355 mm, and a valley is already 10% deep at 3.0 mm. Corrected to 3.4 mm, which is where the slider's 0.1 mm step first crosses the limit
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the profile's x-axis labels overprinted each other into mush.** Ticks were drawn at −halfExtent, −α/2, 0, +α/2, +halfExtent; at the default the middle three sit a few pixels apart and rendered as `−1.00 .001m0.0 mm`, while the right-hand label was clipped by the canvas edge (`20.13 m`). Only the two ends and the centre carry numbers now, end labels are edge-aligned instead of centred, and the ±α/2 lines are named `star A` / `star B` on the plot — dashed for A, dotted for B, so the two are told apart without relying on colour
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the plot titles and the legend went dark-on-dark the moment the light theme was switched on.** Both canvases are a deliberate dark field in both themes, but their DOM overlays inherited `var(--muted)` / `var(--text)`, which flip to slate-900 in light theme — the panel titles and both legend rows became unreadable over a black canvas. Pinned to light ink, with the legend pill's border and background pinned to match. The related latent defect — both canvases read `--panel-bg` from `document.documentElement` while the theme class lands on `document.body`, so `getPropertyValue('--panel-bg')` returned `#060E11` in light theme while `document.body` returned `#e5e7eb` — was removed by making the dark field explicit and documented rather than accidentally correct
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the two sources were a bare `A` and `B`, and the Airy disc was never named.** The scene now labels `star A` / `star B` (with a collision nudge when the pair is within 28 px), captions the pair "the two stars are s = 2.00 mm apart, at 10 m", and glosses the central term in the plot title ("each star arrives as an Airy disc, not a point") and again in the sidebar note ("the bright blob a round aperture makes of a point source"). `θ_min` carries its name before its symbol ("smallest resolvable angle"), `min. separation @ 10 m` became "smallest resolvable gap between the stars, at 10 m", and the legend rows match the canvas names
- FIXED [P2][D8] **seven canvas labels and two DOM overlays were below the 12px legibility floor** (the A/B ticks, the separation caption, the scale-bar label, both axis labels, the tick numbers and the ratio line at 11px; `.plot-title` and `.legend` at 11px). All raised to 12px / 12.5px / 13px · verified: smallest measured glyph 12.0px on canvas, 12.5px in the DOM, at both viewports
- FIXED [P2][D8] **three sidebar rows wrapped into broken shapes** — `★ the star control` split across two lines with "control" orphaned, and `3.355` / `mm` split onto separate lines because `.stat-value` had `overflow-wrap:anywhere` inside a flex row with a `flex-shrink:0` label. Label and value roles swapped (label wraps, value never does) · verified: 0 rows with `scrollWidth > clientWidth` at both viewports
- FIXED [P2][D8] **three overlaps introduced by the longer labels, caught at 1024×768 and fixed there**: the separation caption ran under the bottom-left Aperture/Verdict badge (the star-tick clamp is now `h − pad − boxH − 46` instead of `h − 58`), the valley readout collided with the Rayleigh line label (both shortened), and the rotated y-axis label was clipped by the short canvas (shortened to "brightness (peak = 1)")
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — draw the aperture.** The sim is *about* the aperture and never shows one: D is a slider number, and the first link of the causal chain — a finite hole makes a wave spread — is the one link with no picture at all. Sketch: a small circular-aperture inset at the top-left of the scene whose drawn diameter tracks the D slider, with the Airy radius annotated beside it as `θ = 1.22 λ/D`, and a 1:1 line linking the two so halving the hole visibly doubles the disc. This is also the natural home for enhancement (3). (2) **a circular / slit aperture switch.** The Formal panel prints `slit: θ_min = λ/a  ·  circle: θ_min = 1.22 λ/D` and `Engine.wavefront.rayleighResolution` already implements both, but nothing in the sim ever draws a slit — so the 1.22 is asserted, never earned. Sketch: a two-button selector; `shape:'slit'` in the two `rayleighResolution` calls, `singleSlitIntensity` in place of `airyIntensity` in both the bitmap and the profile, and the 2-D pattern becomes horizontal bands instead of rings so the shape change is visible; the Rayleigh reference line moves from 73.5% to 81.1% (the oracle's sinc² value), which is itself the lesson. (3) **a magnification control that demonstrably fails.** The sim's declared misconception target is "if I can't tell them apart, I need more magnification", and it is currently refuted only in card text — a zoom slider that scales the drawn pattern while the valley percentage and the verdict do not move would refute it in pixels, which is what the misconception needs. (4) **telescope / microscope modes.** JEE examines the microscope form (d_min = 1.22λ/(2n sin β) = 0.61λ/NA, and the oil-immersion 1.5× gain) as often as the telescope form, and none of it is reachable; arcseconds (1 rad = 206265″) and a naked-eye preset (D = 2.5 mm → θ_min ≈ 1′) would anchor the abstraction. (5) **show each star's own pattern.** Drawing I₁ and I₂ as two faint curves under the bold sum would make "incoherent sources add as intensities" visible rather than stated, and would stop students reading the double hump as one pattern with two maxima. (6) **align the two panels' x-axes pixel-for-pixel.** They now share an angular window but not a mapping, and the scene fits to `min(w,h)` so the pattern uses a fifth of a 1150 px-wide canvas; an aspect-aware fit plus a shared x-mapping would let a bright ring sit directly above its bump
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the two stars are drawn warm and cool at a single λ — this is identity coding, not a wavelength claim, and is now disclosed by the legend ("star A (warm)" / "star B (cool)") while the profile curve carries the true spectral colour of λ. The profile's per-frame renormalisation to a unit peak is disclosed on the y axis ("peak = 1"). The faint outer rings clip at the frame edge at the default aperture — the central disc, which is the physics, is fully inside. The shared Feedback pill overlaps the last sidebar row at 1024×768 and is platform chrome. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (all 21 `ctx.font` sites are literal px strings); pause-desync (`timeless:true`, no transport, every repaint is slider-driven, and `draw()` is not gated on `onFrame`); stale canvas after Reset (`__fullReset` → `inqShow(0)` → `onStep(0)` → `draw()`; measured scene mean 7.60 → 5.98 → 7.59); unbounded KaTeX retry (bounded by the `typeof katex === 'undefined'` guard on four one-shot renders); view-only buttons missing `data-no-reset` (both guide buttons carry it); affine/clamped encodings (the one clamp, the γ tone map, is now disclosed); temporal aliasing (nothing moves in time). The only console entry at either viewport, either theme, either DPR is the harness's `/favicon.ico` 404, which does not reappear on reload
- Gates: syntax ✓ (7/7 inline blocks, `node --check`) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay; guided-inquiry 4 cards; controls-tutorial 9 steps — this one caught a real regression when the staging first reused `.cg-hidden`, and passes on the dedicated `.stage-hidden` class) · user-version-eval ✓ (`all 1 builds pass`) · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check ✓ (the repo script enumerates a hardcoded QM list and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display comparison was run directly against `git show HEAD:…` at dpr 1 AND dpr 2: scene 1.000/0.999 and 2.000/1.999, profile 1.000/0.999 and 2.000/1.997, drift 0.00% on all four) · copyright ✓ · `__audit` invariant unchanged (θ_min·D spread = 0 over 12 apertures)
- S3: re-push required for this file.

## C192 ac-through-r-l-and-c-separately — deep review 2026-09-11
- File: `Module-12-EMI-AC-and-EM-Waves/C192-ac-through-r-l-and-c-separately.html` (jee-sim-deep-review; D1 blind spec written before any implementation reading; Track P + Track D incl. D8 at 1500×950 AND 1024×768 + Track L)
- Track P: grid 147 checks (ω ∈ {30,100,314,500,1000,1414,2000} × V_rms ∈ {1,10,20}, incl. ω₀ = 1/√(LC) = 1000 where X_L = X_C = 100 Ω) — **0 mismatches** against an independent Node oracle, pre- and post-fix. `__audit` invariants pass. Phase verified IN PIXELS by trace cross-correlation: i_L drawn at −91.5° (LAGS) and i_C at +90.3° (LEADS) against v, i_R at 0° — the CIVIL/ELI-the-ICE-man signs are correct on screen, and the T/4 markers land at exactly ±0.25 period. browser-verified: yes.
- Track D report card: V:4 C:3 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:2 R:2 L:2 G:3 N:2).
- FIXED [P0][PHYS/FUNC] **pause-desync**: with the sim paused, moving ω or V_rms left the canvas drawing the DEFAULT frequency while every readout showed the new one — `restartOnParamChange` calls `onReset()` (which draws at ω=314) and then restores the chosen value without a redraw, and the rAF loop only calls `onFrame` while playing. `bindSlider` now draws. · verified: paused, ω 314→1800, drawn i_R amplitude 94.8 px → 164.5 px vs oracle 164.8 px at 1500×950 (60.0 → 96.0 vs 96.3 at 1024×768); the on-canvas `i_pk` no longer contradicts `i_L,rms` in the sidebar
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **light theme was unusable**: `cssVar()` read `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)` while the shell's toggle lands `light-theme` on `document.body`, so all three canvases stayed black under a white page and the legend/title text (which DOES follow the theme) turned dark-on-dark. Reads from `document.body` now, and `.legend`'s hardcoded `rgba(6,14,17,.72)` became `var(--surface)`. · verified: canvas pixel [6,14,17] → [248,250,252] on toggle, legend colour #0f172a on a light pill
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **the shared auto-refitting current axis was undisclosed and contradicted card 4**: ticks were a bare `+ / 0 / −`, so nothing on screen said the axis rescales — yet the drawn resistor amplitude runs 9.0 → 302.5 px (34×) across the ω slider while card 4 says i_R is "unmoved". Each panel now prints the live scale above the plot (`v-axis ±14.1 V · i-axis ±0.473 A (shared by all 3)`, with shorter variants auto-selected to fit a 1024px panel), and card 4 names the trap and points at the i_pk readout as the honest number. · verified: caption numbers track both sliders (V_rms 10→20 doubles both)
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **dead-by-autoscale V_rms slider**: the voltage trace was normalised by its own peak and `commonIMax ∝ V_pk`, so V_rms = 1, 5, 10 and 20 produced pixel-identical traces (ampV 317.5 px, ampI_L 302.5 px at every value). The new axis caption makes the control visibly live; a genuine pixel response needs fixed-reference axes (ENHANCEMENT 3). · verified: measured amplitudes at 4 V_rms values
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **two canvas labels painted on top of each other**: `time →` (baseline TOP at plotY+plotH+6) and `i_pk = … A` (baseline BOTTOM at +20) both started at plotX — 11 px of measured overlap in all three panels at both viewports, destroying both strings; at 1024 the readout also ran into `2T window`. Separate rows now (+6 and +24, both baseline TOP, `desiredBottom` 44 → 48). · verified: overlap 0 px at 1500×950 and 1024×768, 10 px of canvas left below
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **temporal aliasing — the wave appeared to travel BACKWARDS at the default frequency**: the window is always 2 periods wide but `state.t` advanced in real seconds, so one 60 Hz frame moved the phase 300° at ω=314 (aliasing to −60°/frame under an axis reading "time →") and 5.3 whole cycles at ω=2000. The clock now advances in units of the current period (0.5 cycles/s at every ω); playback rate only — every plotted point is still the exact solution at the true ω. · verified: 3.0°/frame at ω = 30, 314, 1000 and 2000; Speed 0.25/1/4 gives 0.75/3/12°/frame with X_L and i_L,rms byte-identical
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak — the reported-not-fixed one**: at the card-3 gate ("predict what each current does as ω rises") the sidebar showed, live and un-gated, rows labelled literally `X_L = ωL` and `X_C = 1/(ωC)` with their values and both currents. `#panel-reactance` is now staged behind `revealIndex() >= 3`, i.e. it appears on card 4 where the copy says to read it. · verified: hidden at steps 0/1/2, VISIBLE at 3/4, lifted by `__cgReveals(99)` in the Controls Guide and by `__freeExplore`, re-staged on `__giResume`
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak in the WELCOME HERO**: the hero canvas drew three bands explicitly lettered **R**, **L**, **C** with their current dots at 0 / −π/2 / +π/2 against the shared voltage — the answer to card 2's "Predict the phase", on the landing page before a mode is chosen — and narration line 2 stated it in words ("the current lags a quarter of a cycle behind it… runs a quarter of a cycle ahead"). Band letters removed; narration line 2 now poses the question. Three offset dot-trains still carry the teaser. · verified: 0 `fillText(b.label` sites remain; welcome screenshot re-checked
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] **one-frame ghost trail**: the panel background was filled with `--surface` = `rgba(6,14,17,.95)`, leaving 5% of the previous frame under the traces — faint ghost sinusoids visible in every screenshot, and permanent while paused. `clearRect` before the fill. · verified: trace-colour pixel count now identical after 1 and after 4 redraws (1844/1844 at 1500, 1077/1077 at 1024)
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] **Reset left the prediction gates answered**: after ↻ Reset, card 2 still carried `data-answered`, all three choices disabled with `.correct`/`.dim` painted and `.predict-eval.show` open — against the file's own "Reset = the state of a fresh page load" contract. `__fullReset` now un-answers every card. · verified: answered → Reset → `answered:false, evalShown:false, disabled:[false,false,false], marks:["choice","choice","choice"]`
- FIXED [P2][FUNC] **`data-no-reset` shield gap**: `#qfbPill` / `#qfbSubmit` / `#qfbCancel` were unshielded buttons, so the capture-phase param watcher treated 💬 Feedback as a parameter button and restarted the run (measured t 1.200 → 0.296). Shields added to the pill and the backdrop. The `restartOnParamChange` shells on every other control were audited live and are intact (`#btn-gi`, `#btn-cg`, `#welcome-overlay`, `cg-callout`, `cg-nav`, all `shell-*`/`inq-*`/`toggle-formal` shell-owned). · verified: t 0.0025 → 0.0031 (no restart)
- FIXED [P1][D8] **novice legibility**: the legend is the sim's only colour key and was 11 px with bare symbols — now 12.5 px reading "voltage v(t)" / "current i_R(t)"; plot titles 11 → 12.5 px; canvas amplitude ticks 10 → 12 px and axis labels 11 → 12 px (the 12 px effective bar); the i_pk readout gained its name in words ("peak current i_pk = 0.141 A", auto-dropped when the panel is too narrow); the phase annotation went from `T/4 lag` to `current lags by T/4` (bold 11 → 12 px, short form as fallback). The top pad is now MEASURED from the real legend rect (+30) instead of a constant, because the glossed legend wraps to two rows at 1024×768 and a fixed 92 px let it sit on the trace. · verified: 0 DOM text below 12.5 px except shell-template chrome; legend bottom 73 px vs padTop 103 at 1500, 100 vs 130 at 1024 — clears at both
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a rotating phasor dial in each panel.** The phase is the whole topic and is currently readable only as a horizontal gap between two curves, which is exactly the reading novices get wrong; a small dial per panel with V pinned on the reference axis and I at 0/−90/+90, rotating in step with the traces and with the angle written as a number, converts "a quarter cycle" into an angle and links the two representations (Ainsworth). Sketch: reuse the existing `padTop` band, draw a 34 px-radius dial at the panel's top-left, phase taken from the same `phase` constant `drawPanel` already has. (2) **an instantaneous-power trace p = v·i with its cycle-average line** — always positive for R, symmetric about zero for L and C; "wattless current" and P = V_rms I_rms cos φ are half of what JEE asks about single-element AC circuits and the sim never mentions power. (3) **fixed-reference or lockable axes** — the honest cure for the dead V_rms slider and the shrinking resistor trace rather than the disclosure applied here; a "Lock axis" toggle pinning the current axis at the ω=ω₀ value, or a log amplitude axis, would let both be seen in pixels (the i_L range 4.71 A → 0.071 A is why a naive fixed axis is impossible). (4) **an X_L / X_C vs ω plot with a live ω marker** — the crossing at ω₀ = 1000 is asserted in card 4 and in a readout but never drawn; one small graph makes "opposite frequency dependence" a single glance and sets up C193/C194. (5) **L and C sliders** — both are hardcoded (0.1 H, 10 µF), so X_L = ωL can only be probed through ω and the L-dependence cannot be tested at all. (6) **a DC preset (ω → 0)** — "a capacitor blocks DC, an inductor passes it" is the canonical exam statement and the slider floor is 30 rad/s.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): `.shell-panel-head h3` (11 px) and `.control-range` (12 px) sit below the D8 13 px sidebar bar but are platform-shared template CSS, not changed in one file. `.plot-title`/`.legend` are held at 12.5 px rather than 13 px deliberately: at 13 px the glossed legend overflows the 214 px panel at 1024×768. At 1024×768 the platform Feedback pill overlaps the last sidebar stat row (ω₀) — platform chrome. At ω = 30 the capacitor current is 0.5 px tall (0.0 px at 1024) — that IS the physics (X_C = 3333 Ω, i_C = 3.0 mA against i_L = 4.7 A) and the new axis caption now states the scale, but ENHANCEMENT 3 is what would make it readable. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (0 occurrences; all 8 sites are literal px, now all ≥12 px), unbounded KaTeX retry (guarded 50 ms poll, 3 renders), Speed changing the answer (scales dt only — X_L and i_L,rms identical at 0.25×/1×/4×), integrator drift (closed-form cosines, no integration), affine/clamped encodings in the traces. The only console entry at any of the 147 grid points, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404. **The three plot canvases live in a CSS grid row and are VEILED (`.cg-veiled`, visibility) by the Controls Guide, never `display:none`** — that contract is intact and the new `.stage-hidden` (display:none) is applied ONLY to `#panel-reactance`, a flex child of the aside with no canvas.

## C193 series-lcr-circuit-and-impedance — deep review 2026-09-11
- FILE: `Module-12-EMI-AC-and-EM-Waves/C193-series-lcr-circuit-and-impedance.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (Track P + Track D incl. D8 + Track L)
- TRACK P: **31 live points, 0 mismatches** — 25 grid points (ω ∈ {200, 300, 500, 700, 900, 995, 1000, 1005, 1100, 1400, 1800, 2500} rad/s at default R, V; R ∈ {2, 10, 30, 60} Ω; V ∈ {50, 230, 400} V; plus 6 cross-extreme combinations) driven through the real sliders and read back from the DOM, against an independent Node oracle (X_L = ωL, X_C = 1/(ωC), Z = √(R²+X²), φ = atan(X/R), cos φ computed as R/Z by a second route, P computed as I²R not as S·cos φ, ω₀ = 1/√(LC), Q = ω₀L/R), plus 6 further states checking the new element-voltage readouts (V_R, V_L, V_C, arithmetic sum, phasor sum). Agreement to displayed precision everywhere, no NaN/Infinity, regime string correct at every point, both `__audit` invariants pass (max residual 9.1e−13). **ω₀ = 1000 rad/s is exactly reachable** ((1000−200)/5 = 160 integer slider steps) and reports `Resonance (Z = R)`. **The engine's `Engine.ac` module is correct and was not touched.** Speed 0.25×/1×/4× leaves every readout identical (`state.t` is a display phase only); `state.t` finite after a long run; Reset restores ω/R/V = 500/10/230, card 1, revealStep 0 · browser-verified: yes
- TRACK D: report card V:4 C:3 R:4 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:3 C:2 R:2 L:2 G:2 N:1) · browser-verified: yes, at 1500×950 and 1024×768, both themes
- TRACK L: browser-verified: yes (5 cards walked from the welcome overlay at both viewports; all 3 gates verified to hold Next disabled and to leak nothing pre-commit and to resolve on a wrong answer as well as a right one; Finish → `gi-off` + revealStep 99 with everything revealed; Reset → card 1 + revealStep 0 + element-voltage box hidden again; Controls Guide walks all 10 steps and closes; KaTeX renders all 6 formal rows)
- FIXED [P0][DEMO/D8] **every string on the canvas rendered at 10px sans-serif, whatever size the code asked for.** All 11 `ctx.font` sites read `'600 12px var(--font-sans, sans-serif)'` — canvas cannot parse `var()`, so the assignment is REJECTED and the context silently keeps its previous font, which on a fresh context is `10px sans-serif`. Measured live: setting that string on a virgin context leaves `ctx.font === "10px sans-serif"`. Every declared 10/11/12/13px size collapsed to one illegible 10px, below the D8 floor, and the intended visual hierarchy did not exist. Replaced with literal stacks via `fSans()`/`fNums()` · verified by instrumenting the `font` setter through a whole `draw()`: **22 assignments, 0 rejected**, resolving to 12/13/14px DM Sans and 13px JetBrains Mono
- FIXED [P0][DEMO/D8] **the canvas was frozen in dark-theme ink.** `cssVar()` read the custom properties from `document.documentElement` first and only fell back to `body`, but the theme toggle lands `light-theme` on `<body>`, so `:root` kept reporting the dark values and the `||` fallback never fired. Measured: under `body.light-theme`, `cssVar('--muted')` returned `#cbd5e1` while body's own value was `#334155`. Sampling the canvas in light theme, the section-title band came back RGB 198–234 on a `#f8fafc` panel — ≈1.4:1, invisible; both plot frames, the baseline, the Z tag and every bar label went with it. `cssVar()` now reads body first; the two panel frames moved from `--border` (which is a 0.18-alpha hairline in light theme) to `--ink-mute` · verified: the same band now samples RGB 42–93, ≈10:1
- FIXED [P0][FUNC] **the Controls Guide's Next button could not be clicked, at either viewport.** The fixed Feedback pill (`.qfb-pill`, `right:18px; bottom:18px; z-index:99990`) sits exactly on `.cg-nav` (z-index 4000): `document.elementFromPoint` at `#cg-next`'s centre returned the pill's `<span>Feedback</span>` at **every one of the 10 guide steps**. Fixed the way C192 was — park the pill on the left while the guide is open (`html.cg-open .qfb-pill{right:auto;left:18px}`, class toggled in `setCg`) rather than moving the nav, which would break the layout-probe contract · verified: 0 blocked steps, `navBottomGap` still 16px, `layout-probe` ok
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the phase-angle arc swept 360°−|φ| instead of |φ|.** `ctx.arc(A.x, A.y, arcR, 0, -phi, phi<0)` has the anticlockwise flag exactly inverted: the short way from the R base to the hypotenuse is anticlockwise precisely when φ > 0. Measured drawn sweeps: φ = −86.19° → **273.81°**, φ = ±45° → **315°**, φ = +83.16° → **276.84°** — i.e. the one angle this sim exists to draw was drawn as its reflex at every setting, including the shipped default. Flag corrected to `phi > 0` · verified: the same four cases now sweep 86.19 / 45 / 45 / 83.16°, and φ → 0 shrinks to 2° at ω = 990
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **the impedance triangle — the sim's title subject — was a 4 px × 65 px sliver in a 521 × 627 px box, with all four of its labels stacked on the same 120 × 50 px cluster.** The ohms→pixel scale was pinned to a worst case (Z = 503.6 Ω, which only occurs at one end of the ω slider), so at the default ω = 500, R = 10 it put R at 4.34 px, the φ arc at radius **1.74 px**, and the R / φ / X / Z tags on top of one another; the left 55% of the canvas was empty. Replaced with an oscilloscope-style quantized scale: a 1-2-5 ladder picks the ohms-per-square so four grid squares always cover max(R, |X_L−X_C|), **the grid is drawn and its ohm value printed in the section title**, so a range change is announced rather than silent and both legs still share one isotropic scale (φ stays honest). The arc radius gets a 16 px floor (it is an annotation, not a to-scale quantity), the R tag always goes on the side the reactance leg does not occupy, the Z tag is offset along the hypotenuse's outward normal and clamped inside the panel, and `X_L − X_C` is drawn with real subscripts instead of the literal underscores it used to show · verified at 1500×950 and 1024×768: default triangle now 14 × 205 px on a 50 Ω grid; at resonance the grid switches to 5 Ω and the collapsed triangle is a clean 137 px base with the "AT RESONANCE — the upright leg is gone: Z = R, cos φ = 1" banner; no label pair within 14 px at ω = 200 / 500 / 950 / 1000 / 1500 / 2500 × R = 2 / 10 / 60
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/PHYS] **the syllabus's central point — that V_L or V_C alone can exceed the supply — was not representable anywhere in the sim**, although the shipped default already exhibits it. The sim drew an impedance triangle and never a voltage, and carried no current readout at all, while three separate feedback strings asserted "the current SURGES". Added, to the existing Live-readouts panel: `I_rms`, then a staged box giving `V_R`, `V_L`, `V_C`, their **arithmetic** sum and their **phasor** sum, with a gloss on why the two differ. Nothing new is drawn and no control is added · verified against the oracle at 6 states: default ω = 500 → V_C = **306.0 V from a 230 V supply** (X_C = 200 Ω > Z = 150.3 Ω), arithmetic sum 397.8 V, phasor sum exactly 230.0 V; at resonance V_L = V_C = **2.30 kV = Q·V with Q = 10**, arithmetic sum 4.83 kV, phasor sum 230.0 V; at ω = 1000, R = 2, V = 400 → 20.00 kV each against a 400 V supply. **The sim now cannot teach arithmetic addition of the three voltages: both sums are on screen side by side and only one of them equals the supply**
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the predict-gate leak carried over as "reported-not-fixed".** Two vectors, both live. (a) The **welcome overlay**: the blurb read "watch the impedance triangle — *and the power gap* — collapse" and the narration's second line ended "…and every volt ampere the supply delivers finally becomes real work" — that is verbatim the correct choice of the power gate, delivered before the student reaches card 1. Both rewritten to apparatus-only. (b) The **power bars were staged to appear AT the gate card** (`revealStep >= 1`, and the gate is step 1) with the ω slider live beside them, so the prediction was answerable by dragging. Bars moved to `revealStep >= 4` and the element-voltage box to `revealStep >= 3`, both behind the gates that ask about them, both routed through the single `applyStepReveals` indirection so `__cgReveals(99)` / `__freeExplore()` / `__giResume()` all agree · verified step by step at both viewports: at steps 0–3 `barsOnCanvas:false`, at steps 0–2 `vBoxVisible:false`, Next disabled until a choice is clicked, feedback shown and choices locked after, everything revealed at Finish
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **the deck was rewritten to the SME grammar.** The old deck ran 4 cards with ONE question: card 1 carried UI choreography ("Press ▶ if paused") that contradicted the boot state (the sim boots playing), card 2's stem announced its own reveal ("You will now see two power bars appear…"), and card 3 was pure stage direction ("Drag the frequency slider slowly… Watch three things at once"). New deck, 5 cards / 3 gates: FRAME (apparatus only, no UI) → Q1 *can V_C alone exceed the supply* — the naive "the supply is shared out among the three" is the rival mechanism, and it resolves on the default numbers → Q2 *how big is V_L at resonance* — the quantitative Q-magnification inference JEE examines, with "0 V, the reactances cancel" as the distractor that is consistent with everything seen → Q3 *what S and P do as ω → ω₀* — the "power is V·I" misconception kept as a choice → TELL naming the power factor. Terse claim-only choices, every answer resolving on the spot, zero choreography. Also added `V<sub>L</sub>`-style forms to the voiceover normaliser's `X_L` rule so the narration says "V L", not "VL"
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **two undisclosed normalisations, one of which made the Supply-V slider pixel-dead.** (a) The power bars are scaled to `V²/R`; measured at V = 50 / 230 / 400 the bar fractions are **identical to 4 decimal places** (S 0.0476, P 0.0023) — the V slider moved only the printed numbers. The reference is the right one pedagogically (full height = the power this circuit would draw at resonance, so the P bar's height is cos φ × the S bar's) but it was silent, which the skill scores P1. A dashed full-scale rule is now drawn and labelled "full scale = V²/R = 5.29 kW (the power at resonance)". (b) The waveform panel draws both traces at a fixed 42% of half-height while `I_pk` swings **48-fold** across the ω slider (0.68 A at ω = 200 to 32.53 A at resonance). Volts and amperes share no axis, so per-trace normalisation is legitimate — but it was unstated next to copy promising a surge. The panel now says "heights not to scale — only the φ gap is" and each trace carries its own peak (`v — supply voltage, peak 325 V`, `i — current (dashed), peak 2.16 A`), with `I_rms` also in the sidebar · verified at 12 settings
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the legend pill was dark-on-dark in light theme** (`background:rgba(6,14,17,.72)` hard-coded while its text follows `--text` = `rgb(15,23,42)`), and its Z swatch was `#e2e8f0` while Z is drawn in `--ink`. Pill background moved to `var(--surface-soft)`, swatches to `var(--accent)` / `var(--ink)`, and each key now names its quantity in words before the symbol ("Resistance R", "Net reactance X_L−X_C", "Impedance Z", "Phase angle φ" — the phase arc had no key at all) · verified: computed background `rgba(241,245,249,.96)` under `body.light-theme`
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the ω slider's resonance landmark pointed at the wrong frequency.** `<div class="control-range">` is `justify-content:space-between`, so the middle span "ω₀ ≈ 1000" sat at the row's CENTRE — i.e. under ≈1350 rad/s on a 200–2500 track, where ω₀ = 1000 is at 34.8%. A student following the label would tune to the wrong place. Replaced with an absolutely-positioned tick at `left:34.8%`, reading "▲ ω₀ = 1000" (it is exact, not approximate) · verified: the tick now sits over the thumb when ω = 1000 at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][D8] **the voltage and current traces were distinguished by colour alone, and coincided exactly at resonance** — where one simply painted over the other and the panel appeared to have lost a trace at the very setting the sim is about. The current trace is now dashed as well as amber, and its tag says so · also satisfies the colour-redundancy check
- FIXED [P2][D8] **labels ran off the canvas at 1024×768.** The waveform title, the bar-panel title, the full-scale rule and the two under-bar tags were all clipped at the right edge (and the under-bar pair overlapped each other), the four-key legend overprinted the waveform heading, and the "Z = 150.3 Ω" tag was cut off at the left edge at 1500 too. Added a `pickLabel()` that chooses the longest wording that fits at 12px rather than shrinking below the floor, measured `marginTop` from the legend's live bounding box instead of a fixed 60px, capped the legend width so it wraps, and dropped the plot subtitle below 1200px · verified: nothing clipped or overlapping at 1024×768 or 1500×950
- FIXED [P2][FUNC] the fixed Feedback pill covered the last ~54px of the sidebar, which is now where the element-voltage readouts end; `.shell-aside` given 72px of bottom padding so the column scrolls clear of it. Also removed `powerScaleMax()` — dead code that documented an 80 kW scale the sim never used — and two unused colour lookups
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — draw the VOLTAGE phasor diagram beside the impedance triangle.** The numbers now prove that V_L and V_C exceed the supply and that only the phasor sum returns it, but the student still has to take the geometry on trust. Sketch: a second panel, same isotropic-scale treatment as the triangle, drawing V_R along the current direction, V_L at +90° and V_C at −90° from a common origin, then the resultant V_L−V_C, then the tip-to-tail sum closing on the supply phasor — with a "sum as numbers" ghost bar beside it that visibly overshoots. That turns the sim's most examined point from two readouts into one watchable construction, and it is the representation this topic most needs. (2) **draw X_L(ω) and X_C(ω) as two crossing curves.** The whole causal chain is ω → X_L↑ and X_C↓ → their difference; the sim only ever shows the difference, so the cause is invisible and the C (causal salience) score stays at 3. A small inset with the rising line meeting the falling hyperbola at ω₀ would fix it. (3) **a fixed-axis toggle for the power bars**, so the V²/R reference can be swapped for an absolute watt scale and the V slider becomes pixel-live — the normalisation is now disclosed rather than removed. (4) **unlock L and C**, or ship an L/C preset pair: the cards assert ω₀ = 1/√(LC) and Q = ω₀L/R, and the student can vary only R. (5) **an amplitude-honest current trace** behind a toggle, so "the current surges" can be watched as well as read — currently the surge exists only as a printed peak value
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the Supply-V slider still moves no pixels in the power panel, because the panel's reference scales with V — now drawn and labelled rather than silent (enhancement 3 removes it properly). The waveform x-axis is ωt, not seconds, so the on-screen frequency does not change with ω — stated in the panel title. At the default ω = 500 the triangle is genuinely a sliver (R = 10 Ω against |X| = 150 Ω, φ = −86.2°): that is the physics of a Q = 10 circuit, and the grid now lets it be read. The page title and header subtitle ("and the power you do not get") and the Info modal's Misconception row weakly refute one distractor of gate 3; the title is the sim's registered identity and the modal is an opt-in click, so both were left. Shell template chrome below the D8 13px bar (`.control-range` 12px, panel `h3` 11px, Listen 9px, `#inq-auto` 10px) is platform-shared CSS and was not changed in one file. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: **pause-desync** (instrumented `draw` — the shell's `restartOnParamChange` path repaints exactly once per slider input while paused; the canvas shows the resonance callout with the transport stopped), auto-scale cancelling the ω or R control (the triangle's 1-2-5 range is quantized and labelled, and within a range both legs move with ω), affine/clamped encodings, temporal aliasing (`state.t` drives a 0.6 Hz visual scroll, three orders below the frame rate), Speed changing the answer, integrator drift (there is no integrator — every quantity is analytic). The only console entry in either theme at either viewport is the harness's `/favicon.ico` 404
