### 2026-09-11 · `Module-13-Optics/C203-refraction-and-snells-law.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (deep, P + D + D8 + L)

- Track P: grid 108 points (θ₁ ∈ {0,1,5,15,30,45,60,70,80}° × n₂ ∈ {1.33,1.50,2.42} × d ∈ {0.50,1.00,1.37,2.00} m), **0 mismatches** against an independent Node oracle (θ₂ = asin(sin θ₁/n₂), d′ = d/n₂, v = c/n₂); `__audit.at` 12 points 0 mismatches; `__audit.run()` pass. Mechanism-panel rays measured in PIXELS by total-least-squares fit of the amber ray cloud at 18 (n₂, θ₁) states: drawn θ₁ and drawn θ₂ agree with Snell to **≤ 0.06°**, and the angles are drawn from the NORMAL, not the surface. θ₁ = 0 → θ₂ = 0, straight through. Browser-verified: yes.
- Report card — V:4 C:4 R:3 L:4 G:4 N:4.
- FIXED (11):
  - [P0][PHYS/DEMO] **The wavefronts ran backwards.** `dAir = -(i*spacing + phase)` with `phase` growing in t drove the along-ray coordinate NEGATIVE, so the air bars retreated up-beam and the transmitted bars ran back toward the interface — the pivot the sim exists to show played in reverse, contradicting the arrowheads, card 3 ("in air they march forward at c") and the Info modal's E1 row. Now `phase - (i+1)*spacing`, and the medium family `(j*spacing + phase)/n₂` from j = 0 so the bar emerging at the interface hands over continuously. · verified: masked blue-pixel scan of the leading air bar, projection on the propagation direction now rises monotonically −147.8 → −92.6 px across a period and wraps cleanly; rate 24.6 px/s vs 0.35·spacing = 25.8 expected.
  - [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **Light theme unusable — canvas kept a near-black fill under a light page.** Both panels read `--bg` off `document.documentElement` while the theme toggle sets `body.light-theme`, which is the selector that redefines it; meanwhile every ink had already switched to its light-theme (dark) colour, so all labels, the plot titles and both legends were dark-on-dark. Now read off `document.body`. · verified: canvas corner pixel 6,14,17 → 226,232,240, matching `body` background in both themes.
  - [P1][PHYS] **Pool panel drew rays that violate Snell, including rays escaping from beyond the critical angle.** The eye sat at a fixed point, so the in-water angle was whatever the depth made it (43° at d = 0.5 m) while the construction obeys tan θ_air = n·tan θ_water — Snell only for small angles. Measured errors up to **32°** (glass, d = 0.5 m, where the drawn ray was 0.2° from TIR) and, in diamond (θ_c = 24.4°), **θ_water reached 34°: the panel drew light escaping where no refracted ray exists at all**. The in-water angle is now fixed and capped at min(20°, 0.40·θ_c) with the eye derived from it. · verified: pixel-fitted angles at 15 (n, d) states — worst Snell error **1.64°**, rays drawn beyond θ_c: **0**; d′ still lands exactly on d/n₂.
  - [P1][PHYS] **Formal panel's speed line was a constant reading "v_water ≈ 2.254×10⁸ m/s" with Diamond selected.** Now re-rendered from `Engine.rays.speedInMedium(n₂)` on every medium change and on Reset. · verified: 2.254/1.999/1.239 ×10⁸ m/s for n₂ = 1.33/1.50/2.42, each matching c/n₂; correct again after Reset.
  - [P1][DEMO] **The invariant the sim is named after was never shown with numbers** in the default view (the symbolic equation lives behind ∑ Formal, hidden on boot). A live two-line block now stands in the mechanism panel's top-right quadrant — the one region the incident ray can never enter — reading `n₁ sin θ₁ = 0.707 = 0.707 = n₂ sin θ₂` over `1.00 × sin 45°  and  1.33 × sin 32.1°`. · verified on screen at both viewports, both themes, θ₁ = 0…80°.
  - [P1][DEMO] **No undeviated reference**, so "how much did it bend" had nothing to be measured against. A dashed grey continuation of the incident ray is now drawn into the medium (and at θ₁ = 0 the refracted ray lies exactly on it — card 2's answer, visible). · verified in screenshots at θ₁ = 0/45/80.
  - [P2][D8] Pool panel had **no normal at the exit point**, though card 4 asks the student to read "bends away from the normal" off it. Dashed normal + label added.
  - [P2][D8] At d = 2.00 m the coin **sat on the legend row and its "real coin" label was clipped off the canvas bottom** (`belowSpace = h − ySurf − 24`); reserve widened to 78 px. · verified at 1024×768.
  - [P2][D8] `d′` label was **struck through by the amber ray** at default settings, and `apparent` collided with the `d` bracket. The d′ bracket is now parked clear of the ray (`min(xEye,xExit) − 46`) and the marker reads `apparent coin`, set to the left of its dashed circle.
  - [P2][D8] **`air (n₁ = 1.00)` was struck through by a grazing incident ray** at θ₁ = 80° on a short canvas; moved right of the normal, the one side the incident ray never occupies. Mechanism legend rebuilt from measured text widths as three entries (wavefronts / ray / path with no bend), with a short-form fallback so it cannot overflow at 1024×768; the paraxial note on the pool canvas likewise has a short form (it was cut off mid-word). `.plot-title` / `.plot-inline` 11px → 12px (the only on-screen naming of what each panel is). The lower medium is now named (`water`/`glass`/`diamond`) in the mechanism panel too, not just the pool.
  - [P2][LAYER/DEMO] Card 1's "drag the **angle of incidence** slider … to feel how the refracted ray tilts" invited the student to discover card 2's gated answer (θ₁ = 0 → no bend) before predicting it. Replaced with a description of the boot scene and the new dashed reference — also removing UI-choreography prose the SME grammar forbids.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — an n₁ control.** n₁ is hard-wired to air, so half of refraction is unreachable: the ray can never bend AWAY from the normal, "denser vs rarer" — the essential contrast of the topic — cannot be experienced, and sin θ_c = n₂/n₁ never appears. Sketch: promote the medium `<select>` to a pair (upper / lower), let `computePhysics` pass the chosen n₁ instead of 1.0, and when n₁ > n₂ draw the reflected ray at θ₁ and a θ_c marker arc, with the refracted ray vanishing past it — the engine's `snell()` already returns `totalInternalReflection`, `reflectionAngle` and `criticalAngle` as data, and `updateReadouts` already has the `— (TIR)` branch wired but unreachable. (2) **A θ₂-vs-θ₁ curve** under the mechanism panel with a dot tracking the slider: it makes the non-linearity (and, with (1), the vertical terminus at θ_c) a shape rather than a sequence of numbers. (3) **The partially reflected ray** at θ₁ on the incident side, faint and labelled — refraction is never the whole story at a boundary, and it is the hook into C204. (4) **Stage one bar through its pivot**: highlight a single wavefront and let the student scrub it across the interface, instead of six bars moving at once. (5) **A two-ray paraxial pencil in the pool** so the apparent depth is CONSTRUCTED by two backward extensions crossing, rather than asserted by one dashed line through a point the code already knew. (6) **A protractor overlay** on the mechanism panel so the drawn angles can be measured, closing the loop on the new live-Snell readout.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the pool construction keeps a **1.6° residual** against Snell (it is the paraxial tan-law that makes d′ = d/n exact); this is now disclosed on the canvas ("d′ = d/n is the near-vertical view; the viewing angle is opened up here so the bend shows") and is ~1 line width over the drawn segment. θ₁ stops at 80°, so true grazing incidence is not reachable. Shell chrome `.shell-panel-head h3` / `.aside-zone-head h3` at 11px sit below the D8 13px bar but are platform-shared template CSS, not changed in one file. On-canvas angle labels are `θ₁=45°` / `θ₂=32.1°` without their words; the words are on the sidebar controls and readouts in the same frame. At 1024×768 the Readouts panel falls below the fold with the inquiry open — mitigated, since both angles and both depths are also drawn on the canvases. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (all 7 sites literal px), pause-desync (every control handler repaints directly; paused canvas verified frozen without input and repainting on input), Speed changing the answer (1×/0.5× bar advance 25.9/13.1 px/s, exactly ∝ speed, readouts byte-identical at every speed), auto-scale cancelling a control (no auto-scale; the pool's px/m is fixed by the 2.0 m slider maximum), affine/clamped encodings (drawn θ₂ = computed θ₂ to 0.06° across 18 states), temporal aliasing (bar phase 25.8 px/s against a 73.7 px period — ~2.9 s per cycle), WELCOME HERO leak (the hero fans 10/25/40/55/70°, never 0°, so card 2's answer is not shown), predict-gate leak at either gate (nothing on screen states the 0° case pre-commit; `Next` disabled and `.predict-eval` hidden until answered), unbounded KaTeX retry (the retry is the pre-existing 50 ms `renderMath` poll, unchanged). Only console entry at any state, either theme, either viewport: the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

## C210 the-astronomical-and-terrestrial-telescope — deep review 2026-09-11
- File: `Module-13-Optics/C210-the-astronomical-and-terrestrial-telescope.html` (jee-sim-deep-review; D1 blind spec written from title + curriculum entry BEFORE any implementation reading; Track P + Track D incl. D8 at 1500×950 AND 1024×768 + Track L)
- Scope note: the file is named for curriculum concept **210, "The astronomical & terrestrial telescope"**, but the sim built into it is *"Telescope and Microscope — two lenses, two opposite jobs"* (concepts 209 + half of 210). **The terrestrial telescope — the erecting lens, L = f_o + 4f + f_e, the image restored upright — does not exist anywhere in the file.** That gap is ENHANCEMENT 1.
- Track P: grid 25 points (f_o ∈ {0.20, 0.50, 1.00, 1.20, 1.50} m × f_e ∈ {0.020, 0.035, 0.050, 0.075, 0.100} m, the full slider box incl. all four corners) — **0 readout mismatches** against an independent Node oracle, pre- and post-fix: `M_tel = −f_o/f_e`, `L_tel = f_o + f_e` and `M_mic = −(L/f_o)(D/f_e)` are implemented exactly. Every P0/P1 below is a **drawn-pixel** defect, not an arithmetic one — the numbers were right and the picture contradicted them. Drawn geometry re-verified IN PIXELS: the emergent bundle's measured slope is now tan α′ = 0.120 / 0.600 / 1.167 / 3.000 / 2.611 / 4.500 against the oracle 0.120 / 0.601 / 1.201 / 3.004 / 2.575 / 4.505 at (f_o,f_e) = (0.2,0.1), (0.5,0.05), (1.0,0.05), (1.0,0.02), (1.5,0.035), (1.5,0.02) — upward at every point. browser-verified: yes.
- Track D report card: V:4 C:4 R:2 L:4 G:4 N:4 (pre-fix V:2 C:2 R:2 L:2 G:2 N:2). **R stays at 2 after the fixes and that is deliberate**: the one regime a compound microscope needs (f_o ≪ L) is outside the slider range by construction, and the terrestrial regime does not exist — ENHANCEMENTS 1 and 2 are what move it.
- FIXED [P0][PHYS] **the eye-angle α′ was computed on ANGLES instead of TANGENTS.** `alphaOut = (f_o/f_e)·α` with α = 0.06 rad ran past 90° for every f_o/f_e > 26.2 and past 180° above 52.4, so `tan(alphaOut)` changed sign and **the emergent bundle was drawn on the WRONG side of the axis at 5 of 25 grid points** (f_o=1.0/f_e=0.02, 1.0/0.035, 1.2/0.035, 1.5/0.035, 1.5/0.05 — the telescope's inversion story drawn backwards), while the printed label read `α′ = 257.8°` at f_o = 1.5 m, f_e = 0.020 m. Now `tan α′ = (f_o/f_e)·tan α`, which is not an approximation here but the exact geometry of the drawn ray (h_image/f_e through the eyepiece centre). · verified: pixel-measured emergent slope vs oracle at 6 (f_o,f_e) points, agreement ≤3%, upward at all; label 257.8° → 77.5°
- FIXED [P0][PHYS] **the microscope bench drew an arrangement that cannot exist, at 100% of grid points.** The object distance came from `u = f_o·L/(L − f_o)` with L = 0.16 m fixed and f_o ≥ 0.20 m, so u is **negative everywhere in range** (−0.190 m on the defaults): the "object" arrow was drawn to the RIGHT of its own objective, *inside* the tube, and `hImg = −h_obj·(v/u)` came out POSITIVE so the arrow captioned "real image (inverted)" was drawn pointing UP. Both were false pictures. The bench is now an explicitly captioned SCHEMATIC of the standard arrangement — object left of the objective (upright), real image up the tube (inverted, below the axis), eyepiece one f_e beyond — in which the one quantitative element is the image HEIGHT, set by the true m = −L/f_o and printed beside it, so the f_o slider still carries the correct functional form. The canvas now states the scale ("schematic — lens spacings nominal; the image HEIGHT carries the true m = −L/f_o") and, whenever |M| < 1 (14 of 25 grid points, **including the defaults at −0.80×**), says so in words. · verified: object left of objective and image arrow below the axis at every grid point; |M|<1 caption appears at (1.0, 0.05) and is absent at (1.5, 0.02) where |M| = 1.33. **The root cause — one shared f_o cannot be both a telescope objective (0.2–1.5 m) and a microscope objective (2–20 mm) — is not fixable by drawing and is ENHANCEMENT 1/2.**
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the "parallel rays from a distant star" were drawn parallel to the AXIS and then focused OFF the axis.** The two marginal rays were horizontal (α = 0) while only the chief ray was tilted, yet all three converged at f_o·tan α below the axis — an axis-parallel bundle focuses ON the axis. Measured pre-fix: the marginal rays sat at y = 193.0 and 237.0 at BOTH x = 65 and x = 95, i.e. zero slope. The whole bundle now carries the same tan α. · verified: at x = 95 the three rays sit at 193.6 / 215.6 / 237.6 and at x = 20 at 189 / 211 / 233 — one parallel bundle at 3.4°
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the α arc was drawn in the wrong quadrant.** `arc(xO, telY, 22, π−α, π)` sweeps BELOW the axis (canvas +y is down) while the incident ray arrives from ABOVE it, so the marked angle was in empty space. Now `π → π+α`. The same error had the α′ arc sweeping downward under an upward bundle — now `−α′ → 0`. · verified in both screenshots at both viewports
- FIXED [P1][PHYS/DEMO] **the ray path broke at the eyepiece and the bundle left the frame in 80 px.** The emergent rays were started at an invented `exitSpread = ±14 px` about the axis while the incident rays actually arrived at y ≈ telY+35 — a ~20 px visible discontinuity through the lens — and then ran to the right margin, crossing y = 0 at x ≈ 808 of a 1150 px canvas so the right 30% of the telescope bench was empty. Each ray now leaves from where it actually met the eyepiece (the emergent pencil is correspondingly narrow, which is the real exit pupil and is now stated on the canvas) and is clamped to the bench's own band. · verified: continuous path at all 6 sampled (f_o,f_e); bundle terminates inside the frame and below the legend at every setting
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the microscope's emergent bundle swept diagonally across the divider and through the telescope diagram.** `yEnd = y ± tan β · outDx` with no bound put microscope rays at y = 316 and 336 on a bench whose axis is at y = 555, i.e. 70 px ABOVE the telescope's own axis — measured by colour scan at x = 700. Clamped to h/2 + 14. · verified: 0 microscope-ray pixels above the divider at 1500×950 and 1024×768
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the telescope's intermediate REAL IMAGE was a 3 px dot.** It is the hinge of the whole instrument (the objective makes it; the eyepiece is a magnifier *on it*), inquiry card 4 asserts "the arrows are drawn flipped in the scene above", and the Info modal's K3 principle claims "the intermediate real image [is] rendered" — none of which was true: there was no arrow on the telescope bench at all, and the microscope's was drawn upright. Now an inverted arrow of height f_o·tan α from the axis to F′, captioned "F′ — real image of the star, upside down (height f_o·tan α)", with the caption flipping to whichever side of F′ has room. · verified: card 4's claim now reproduces on both benches
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **the light theme never reached the canvas.** `cssVar()` read `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)` while the shell's toggle lands `light-theme` on `document.body` and the light palette is declared on `body.light-theme` — so the scene stayed pinned to the dark `:root` values: canvas corner pixel [6,14,17] under a page background of rgb(226,232,240). Reads from `document.body` now. · verified: corner pixel [6,14,17] → [229,231,235] on toggle
- FIXED [P2][DEMO] **contrast regression the theme fix exposed** — five hard-coded `#e2e8f0` object/image arrows and captions became invisible the moment the canvas correctly followed the light palette. All now take `cssVar('--ink')`. · verified: object arrow, F′ arrow and both captions legible in the light screenshot
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak in the WELCOME HERO — the named class.** The landing hero animated `|M_tel| = f_o/f_e` RISING and `|M_mic| = (L/f_o)(D/f_e)` FALLING against f_o in two colours — literally the answer to inquiry card 2 ("what does a LONGER objective do to the two magnifications?") — while the blurb stated it in words ("A long objective helps a telescope and hurts a microscope") and narration line 2 read it aloud ("so a longer objective helps… so a longer objective hurts"). The hero now draws the telescope itself (parallel bundle in, converge at F′, wider parallel bundle out) at ONE setting and leaks nothing; the blurb and narration pose the question instead of answering it. · verified: hero paints 14 570 opaque px, blurb/narration re-read, and the card-2 gate shows `evalShown:false` until a choice is clicked
- FIXED [P1][D8] **novice legibility of the scene.** Symbols were mixed and unglossed: the canvas wrote `f₀` (subscript ZERO) while every sidebar control says f_o (letter o), and `f_e` with a raw underscore; α and α′ were bare Greek letters with no names anywhere on screen. Now consistent `f_o` / `f_e` throughout the canvas, and words before symbols on first appearance — "α = 3.4° angle the star makes at the objective", "α′ = 50.2° angle the SAME star makes at your eye", "bundle leaves parallel → image at infinity, eye relaxed", "Objective (long focus)", "Eyepiece (short focus)", "object (upright)", "real image, upside down", "tube length L = f_o + f_e", and `M = α′/α = −f_o/f_e = −20.0×  (minus sign = image upside down)` so the sign is read, not decoded. Legend 11 → 12.5 px with fuller names. · verified: smallest DOM glyph in the plot 12.5 px; canvas text 12 px; every arrow, arc and colour named in place (Mayer contiguity) rather than in a distant key
- FIXED [P2][D8] **label collisions at both viewports.** Pre-fix, the microscope bench was an unreadable pile at 1024×768 ("real image (inverted)" / "object" / "Objective f₀" / "Eyepiece f_e" mutually overprinted) and the legend pill overlapped the telescope's tube-length bracket. Lens labels, the F′ caption, the α/α′ captions and the two M badges now sit on separate measured baselines; the α′ captions moved BELOW the axis because the wedge above it belongs to the emergent bundle; the section label moved to telY−100; the legend went back to three items (the two colours it had gained are now labelled on the objects themselves) and to top:36px. · verified: no overlapping text at 1500×950 or 1024×768 at f_o ∈ {0.20, 1.00, 1.50} × f_e ∈ {0.020, 0.050, 0.100}
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] **copy that contradicted the build.** Card 3 said to grab the slider "in the panel below" — the Controls panel is in the right sidebar at every width ≥1000 px. Info-modal K2 claimed "telescope and microscope use ONE horizontal pixels-per-metre scale so their tube lengths are directly comparable by eye", which stops being true once the microscope bench is an honest schematic, and K3 claimed the intermediate real image was drawn when it was not. All three corrected. The sidebar Readouts note now states the |M_mic| < 1 consequence instead of leaving a "microscope" reading −0.80× unexplained.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — replace the microscope bench with the TERRESTRIAL telescope.** It fixes the P0 root cause and delivers the half of C210 that is missing. Both benches then share one honest optical axis and one realistic parameter box (f_o 0.2–1.5 m, f_e 20–100 mm), because both are telescopes. Sketch: keep the top bench; on the bottom bench place the objective at xO, the intermediate real image at f_o, an **erecting lens of focal length f** (new slider, 0.02–0.15 m) at 2f beyond that image, its 1:1 inverted re-image at a further 2f, and the eyepiece one f_e beyond that; draw `L = f_o + 4f + f_e` as a bracket against the top bench's `f_o + f_e` at the SAME px/m scale so the 4f penalty is visible as length; the final arrow is drawn UPRIGHT while the top bench's is inverted, and both print the same |M| = f_o/f_e — the whole point of the erecting lens being that it costs tube length and buys orientation, not magnification. Cards 2/4 become a prediction about ORIENTATION and tube length rather than about two instruments that cannot share a lens. (2) **if the microscope is to stay, give it its own objective.** A second slider `f_o(microscope)` over 2–20 mm with the telescope's f_o left alone makes both instruments real at once and lets card 2's "opposite jobs" claim be *demonstrated* rather than asserted; the shared-scale drawing would then need two declared scales (metres on top, millimetres below) with a scale bar on each. (3) **a "through the eyepiece" inset.** The topic's payoff observable — a real scene, magnified and INVERTED (astronomical) or upright (terrestrial) — is never shown; a 140 px inset showing a simple star field / distant tree at angular size α, redrawn at α′ and flipped, would convert the sign of M from an annotation into something seen. (4) **normal adjustment vs near-point toggle.** JEE examines both forms (M = −f_o/f_e vs −(f_o/f_e)(1 + f_e/D), L = f_o + f_e vs f_o + f_e D/(f_e+D)) and only the relaxed-eye case exists; a two-state toggle would move the eyepiece and change both readouts, with the emergent bundle becoming convergent-to-D instead of parallel. (5) **objective aperture.** `Engine.rays.telescope` already returns `lightGatheringRatio = (D/d_pupil)²` and it is never surfaced; an aperture slider driving the drawn lens height, a brightness swatch and the resolving-power link to C216 would separate "magnification" from "light grasp", which is the single most common student conflation about telescopes. (6) **Galilean telescope preset** (concave eyepiece, L = f_o − f_e, erect, no real image formed) — the third textbook form, and the cleanest contrast for "where is the intermediate image?".
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the telescope bench occupies only the left ~25% of the frame at f_o = 0.20 m — that is the honest consequence of a single px/m scale sized for f_o = 1.50 m, and the tube bracket reads the true 0.300 m. The emergent pencil is ~3 px wide because f_e ≪ f_o (the real exit pupil); "parallel out" is therefore carried by a caption rather than by visible ray separation. At f_o = 1.50 m the microscope's drawn image is at its 3 px floor (true m = −0.11×) with the number printed beside it. `.shell-panel-head h3` (11 px) and `.control-range` (12 px) are platform-shared template CSS and were not changed in one file. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: `ctx.font` with `var()` (the single `ctx.font` site is a literal `'12px '` + family); pause-desync (`timeless:true`, no transport in the markup, every repaint is slider-/reset-/step-driven and `drawScene()` is not gated on `onFrame`); unbounded KaTeX retry (guarded 50 ms poll, 4 one-shot renders); view-only buttons missing `data-no-reset` (both guide buttons and the welcome overlay carry it); stale canvas after Reset (verified f_o 0.30/f_e 0.09 → Reset → 1.00/0.050, readout −20.0×, card 1 restored); Speed changing the answer (no transport — nothing evolves in time); temporal aliasing (nothing moves in time). **The sibling-sim failure the brief warned about — two lens groups drawn with independent auto-fits breaking the shared axis — is NOT present: both benches take the same `pxPerM = usableW/1.75` and each has its own straight principal axis; the defect found here was the inverse, one bench's rays invading the other's axis, now clamped.** The only console entry at any grid point, either theme, either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.

### 2026-09-11 · `Module-13-Optics/C208-prism-deviation-and-dispersion.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (deep, P + D + D8 + L)

- TRACK P: **745-point grid, 0 mismatches** against an independent Node oracle (sin i = n sin r₁, r₁ + r₂ = A, n sin r₂ = sin e, δ = i + e − A, δ_m from sin i_m = n sin(A/2), θ_c = asin(1/n), i_min from sin i_min = n sin(A − θ_c), Cauchy n = 1.5 + 4e−15/λ²). 120 readout checks (A ∈ {30,40,50,60,70} × i ∈ 12 values × mono/white), 45 angle-chain checks (r₁, r₂, e, δ and the identities r₁ + r₂ = A, δ = i + e − A), 567 drawn-geometry checks over the whole (i, A) plane, and 13 full Snell audits of the **drawn pixels** via a canvas path recorder. The closed-form δ_m agrees with a 0.0005°-resolution numerical minimisation of δ(i) to 1e-4 at every A, and the numeric minimum sits at i = e = i_m (A = 60° → δ_m = 38.3328° at i = 49.167°, r₁ = r₂ = 30.000°); n back-solved from sin((A+δ_m)/2)/sin(A/2) returns 1.513223 = n(550 nm). Violet (n = 1.525000) deviates more than red (n = 1.508163) at every grid point; the drawn fan is red-on-top / violet-below, i.e. violet bent further toward the base — **colour order correct, not reversed**. browser-verified: yes, pre- and post-fix.
- TRACK D report card: V 4 · C 4 · R 4 · L 4 · G 4 · N 4 (pre-fix V 1 · C 1 · R 2 · L 2 · G 2 · N 2).
- FIXED [P0][PHYS/DEMO] **the ray path was drawn on the wrong side of both faces, and at the DEFAULT state no refracted ray was drawn at all.** `nLeft`/`nRight` were built as `(sin θ, −cos θ)` / `(−sin θ, cos θ)`, which for an apex-up triangle are the INWARD normals, while every use treated them as outward; the internal direction therefore pointed out of the glass, `intersectRaySeg` found no second-face hit, and at i = 45°, A = 60° the scene showed a single white line entering from below-right, crossing the prism body, and stopping dead at the entry face — no internal ray, no emergent ray, no δ arc. Normals flipped to genuinely outward and the rotations corrected to −i / −r₁ with the exit direction taken from the outward exit normal. · verified: canvas path recorder at 13 configurations — `i_drawn` = i, `r₁_drawn` = asin(sin i/n), `r₂_drawn` = |A − r₁|, `e_drawn` = |e|, `δ_drawn` = δ, all to 3 dp (45/60: 45.000, 27.858, 32.142, 53.616, 38.616); 567-point (i, A) sweep, every emerging state draws exactly incident + internal + emergent and every TIR state draws incident + internal + reflected, with the reflected ray at 180° − r₂ from the outward normal (law of reflection).
- FIXED [P0][FUNC/DEMO] **moving either slider silently reverted White light to Monochromatic and wiped the δ–i curve.** The shell's default `restartOnParamChange:true` routes every slider `input` through `restartWithCurrentParams` → `onReset`, and the mono/white pair is a *button* mode that the snapshot-and-restore cannot hold, so `onReset` put `state.mode='mono'` back and `plotPoints.clear()` ran on every single input event — the plot could never hold more than the reset point plus the current one, though card 2 says "the δ–i plot below records every setting". `cfg.restartOnParamChange:false` (the documented opt-out for a slider-driven diagram; this sim has no run to restart). · verified: after clicking White, an i nudge and an A nudge both leave `#btnWhite.active` and 4214 coloured scene pixels (was 195, i.e. mono); dragging i from 25° to 85° now paints accent pixels in **928 distinct plot columns** spanning i = 28.9° (exactly the oracle's i_min, below which nothing emerges) to 85°, with an interior screen-y maximum — a real U-curve — where before the same drag left a 2-point stub.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO/D8] **canvas palette read from `documentElement` while the theme class lands on `body`.** `--text`/`--muted`/`--accent`/`--border` are overridden by `body.light-theme`, so in light theme both canvases kept the DARK values: every scene label and the plot's entire axis labelling (all tick numbers, both axis titles, the grid) rendered near-white on a white panel. `getComputedStyle(document.body)` in `drawScene` and `drawPlot`. · verified: light-theme screenshot at 1500×950 — all labels and ticks dark and legible; dark theme unchanged.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the plot's fixed δ floor of 20° clipped the minimum off the bottom at small apex angles.** δ_m = 16.11° at A = 30° and 22.34° at A = 40°, and points outside [20°, 80°] are dropped and the δ_min guide suppressed — so at the low end of the A slider the dip, the thing the graph exists to show, was simply absent. Floor → 10°; the whole reachable range (δ ∈ [16.1°, 66.5°] over i ∈ [20°, 85°] × A ∈ [30°, 70°]) now fits. The axis stays FIXED deliberately — auto-fitting would cancel the very dependence the A slider teaches.
- FIXED [P1][FUNC] **one-frame ghost in both canvases**: they were "cleared" by filling with the panel background `rgba(6,14,17,0.95)`, leaving 5 % of the previous frame — the previous δ label was still legible under the new one after a mode change. `clearRect` before the fill, ×2. · verified: magnified crop of the emergent-ray region, residue gone; the path recorder now logs exactly 3 ray strokes per input instead of 3 per redraw accumulating.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] **the "At symmetric passage?" readout was one interaction stale** — `updateReadouts()` runs before `drawScene()` in every handler but read `state.highlightSym`, which `drawScene` sets. Symmetry (|r₁ − r₂| < 0.4°) is now decided inside `updateReadouts` from the same `computeRay` result that feeds δ.
- FIXED [P0][LAYER] **predict-gate leak at card 1**, which asks the student to predict whether δ rises, falls, or turns around: the plot drew a labelled `δ_min = 38.33°` guide line across it and the sidebar printed a "Minimum δm" row — the answer, in numbers, on screen, pre-commit. A `state.reveal` step flag (set by `onStep`, lifted to 99 by `__cgReveals`, to 4 by `__freeExplore`) withholds both until step ≥ 1. · verified: at card 1 `plotGreenPx` = 0 and the row is `display:none`; at card 2 they are back (348 px).
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak at card 3**, which asks what the internal ray does at the minimum: card 2's own prose answered it verbatim ("When the ray inside the prism runs **parallel to the base**, the passage is symmetric…"), the scene caption read "parallel to base — δ is MINIMUM", the base-parallel construction line was drawn, and the readout said "YES — parallel to base". Card 2 reworded to point at the observation without making it; the construction line and the words "parallel to base" staged behind reveal ≥ 3, with the neutral caption "δ is at its MINIMUM here" before that. · verified: symOut reads "YES" at cards 2–3 and "YES — parallel to base" from card 4 on.
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **predict-gate leak at card 4** (which colour deviates most): a student who flipped to White at that card saw the canvas note naming violet and an "Angular dispersion (violet − red) = +1.60°" row. The fan still draws — it is the evidence — but the naming labels, the note and the dispersion row are staged behind reveal ≥ 4. · verified: at card 4 with White on, `dispShown` false and the scene carries the fan (5181 coloured px) with no colour names.
- FIXED [P1][LAYER] **WELCOME-HERO leak** — the landing page gave away all three card answers before card 1: blurb "Rotate a prism and δ **dips at symmetric passage** — white light fans **violet more than red**", narration line 2 the same, hero caption "Equal-Δi dots of δ(i)…". All three rewritten to pose the topic instead of resolving it. The Controls-Guide step for the rotation slider ("Sets incidence i. **Watch δ dip, then rise**.") and the readouts step were rewritten too.
- FIXED [P1][D8] **unglossed glyphs**: the dashed entry-face normal, the dashed undeviated continuation at the exit, and the two ends of the spectrum fan carried no identification at all. Added "normal" on the normal, "original direction (no prism)" on the continuation, "angle of incidence i = 45.0°" and "deviation δ = 38.6°" (words before symbols), and "red — deviates least" / "violet — deviates most" anchored on the fan's extreme rays, so the colour distinction is also carried by word and position, not colour alone.
- FIXED [P1][D8/DEMO] **the causal chain was invisible**: the student saw i and δ but never r₁, r₂ or e — the four angles the prism formula is actually built from. Three rows added to the existing Live-readouts panel (no new panel, no new representation). · verified at 45 grid points: r₁ + r₂ = A and δ = i + e − A hold to 0.02°, and e reads "— (total internal reflection)" exactly where the oracle says the ray cannot emerge.
- FIXED [P2][D8] **readout values were wrapping one character per line**: `.stat-value` has `overflow-wrap:anywhere` and no minimum width, so any label over ~200 px squeezed the number to 0–9 px — measured `valW` 0 px / `rowH` 140 px on the new r₁ row and, pre-existing, `valW` 3 px / `rowH` 118 px on "Angular dispersion (violet − red)". Five labels shortened; every visible row now measures `valFits: true` at both 1500×950 and 1024×768. Shell CSS untouched.
- FIXED [P2][D8] the white-mode summary note collided with the "violet — deviates most" label at 1024×768 (the fan always leaves down-and-right) — moved to the bottom-left; and the minimum-deviation caption sat ON the internal ray it describes — raised 14 px.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a refractive-index / material control, and the prism formula solved live.** Concept 208's actual JEE experiment is *determining n of a prism from δ_m* (`n = sin((A+δ_m)/2)/sin(A/2)`, curriculum entry 243), and with n frozen at crown glass the student can never run it, never move θ_c = 41.36°, and never reach the A > 2θ_c regime where nothing emerges at any incidence. Sketch: a `Material` select (water 1.33 / crown 1.52 / flint 1.62 / dense flint 1.72) driving `CAUCHY_A` with `CAUCHY_B` kept so dispersion scales, plus an "n measured from δ_m" readout that substitutes the live A and δ_min into the formula and visibly returns the n you selected — the experiment closing on itself. (2) **draw r₁ and r₂ as labelled arcs at the two faces** — both normals are already drawn, so the arcs are the missing half of the causal chain and would make it visual rather than numeric (`arcShort` between each face normal and the internal ray, drawn only when the radius fits inside the glass). (3) **an i ⇄ e reversibility control** — the two branches of δ(i) encode the JEE fact that i and e are interchangeable, and the sim never shows the two incidences that give the same δ. (4) **a decaying ghost of the last few emergent rays**, so approaching the minimum reads as a visible swing-and-reverse instead of a memory comparison. (5) **replace the welcome hero's δ(i) dot-plot with a prism-and-ray sketch** — the copy is now neutral but the picture still shows the dip before card 1 (a renderer change, hence not fixed here). (6) **a screen/detector at the right edge plus a magnified inset of the fan** — the angular dispersion is 1.60° at the default, about 15 px of spread at the canvas edge: correct, but small.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the ⓘ Info modal states the concept and the answer, as it does in every sim in this corpus — meta-UI, explicitly skipped by the Controls Guide. The auto-sweep runs i over 22°–82° rather than the slider's full 20°–85°, which is harmless (it crosses the minimum at every A: 23.06° at A = 30° up to 60.22° at A = 70°) and keeps the sweep off the TIR dead zone at both ends. At 1024×768 the emergent ray is clipped by the canvas edge (fixed 400 px ray length) — normal for a ray diagram. A > 2θ_c = 82.7° is outside the A slider, so "no light emerges at any incidence" is not demonstrable without ENHANCEMENT 1. Named defect classes checked explicitly: `ctx.font = 'var(--font-sans)'` **not present** (both sites interpolate the resolved computed value; all canvas text 12–13 px, DPR-safe); canvas theme from `documentElement` — **was present, fixed**; pause-desync — not present (every control handler calls `drawScene`/`drawPlot` directly, and with the sim paused an i change measurably repaints); auto-scale cancelling a control — not present (both plot axes are fixed constants; the A slider visibly translates the curve); affine/clamped encodings — not present (the drawn angles ARE the computed angles, proven by the path recorder); temporal aliasing — not applicable (closed-form, no integration); Speed changes playback only (12.00 °/s at 1×, 23.99 °/s at 2×, δ(i) untouched); Reset restores i = 45°, A = 60°, mono and clears the curve; `data-no-reset` shields intact. The only console entry at any grid point, in either theme, at either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (7/7 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay, guided-inquiry 5 cards, controls-tutorial 12 steps — all PASS; run without `--baseline`, no git operations permitted in this loop) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check — the repo script sweeps a hardcoded QM directory and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display ratio was measured directly instead: `#scene` 1150×518 on 1150×518.2 (1.000 / 0.9997) and `#plot` 1150×323 on 1150×323.8 (1.000 / 0.9974) at 1500×950, and 674×382 on 674×382.2 / 674×238 on 674×238.8 at 1024×768 — no drift, and no CSS was touched ✓ · copyright ✓

Measured live: proton at 100 eV returned 1.226e-10 m (identical to the electron) instead of 2.862e-12 m; the cricket ball returned 6.894e-19 m / ratio 9.85e-18 / "17.0 orders of magnitude apart" against card 2D's "1.7 × 10⁻³³ m", card 2D's "~10⁻³²" and card 3's "~32 orders" — the sim's own namesake conclusion was false on screen. Fixed by passing `mass` inside the options object (and teaching the fallback stub to read it too). Post-fix: 1.70 × 10⁻³³ m, 2.43 × 10⁻³², 31.6 orders — oracle 1.6990e-33 / 2.4271e-32 / 31.615.
- FIXED [P0][DEMO] **canvas theme read from `documentElement` while the shell sets `body.light-theme`** (`css()`, L2115). - FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the Davisson–Germer headline was neither the default nor reachable.** The KE slider stepped 0.1 dex, so the student jumped 49.58 eV → 62.42 eV straight past 54 eV, and the boot state was 100 eV (peak 34.8°). Electron preset is now 54 eV (= 54 V accelerating pd), step 0.01; boot state reads λ = 1.67 × 10⁻¹⁰ m and the plot annotates θ ≈ 50.9° — oracle 12.27/√54 = 1.6697 Å, asin(1.669/2.15) = 50.919°, against the historically measured 50°. Geometry audited for the classic factor-of-two error: the sim uses the surface-grating form `d sinθ = nλ` with d = 2.15 Å and θ from the normal, which is the NCERT convention and is equivalent to 2d′sinθ_gl with d′ = 0.91 Å, θ_gl = 65° (both give 1.65 Å) — **no factor-of-two error present**.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] **the intensity model went dead over two decades and then jumped.** Peak height was multiplied by `min(1, 3λ/d)`, so for 10⁻³ < λ/d < 1/3 the curve drew at ≤10% of the frame (a proton at λ/d = 0.013 showed a flat line) under a "DIFFRACTION VISIBLE" tag, and at λ/d = 10⁻³ it switched branch and jumped discontinuously to a full-height spike. Replaced the two duplicated branch stacks with one `scatteredIntensity()` in which the λ/d dependence is carried by the peak WIDTH, not the height — a shorter wavelength packs the orders into a narrower forward beam instead of making the crystal stop scattering. Verified continuous: peak height 1.000 → 1.2 monotonically across λ/d = 1.5 → 10⁻³⁰, no branch step.
- FIXED [P1][DEMO] the peak was too broad to read as a peak (σ = 0.6·λ/d = 26.7° at the default, 63° FWHM, curve exiting both frame edges at ~30% height). Coefficient 0.6 → 0.30: σ ≈ 13°, the two maxima at ±50.9° are cleanly separated and the curve reaches the axis inside ±90°.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] the canvas label hardcoded "→ nickel crystal" for every setting; at the cricket default it read "Cricket ball → nickel crystal" directly above its own caption "obstacle array (d = 7.00 × 10⁻² m)". Now uses the same `isNickel` test as the caption.
- FIXED [P1][D8] the DOM `.plot-title` ("DIFFRACTION / DAVISSON–GERMER …", absolutely positioned at top:12px) and the canvas particle label at y = 24 overprinted into unreadable glyph soup at both viewports and in both themes. Canvas label moved to y = 46.
- FIXED [P1][PHYS] λ = h/√(2mK) was applied silently across the whole 40-decade KE slider; for an electron the non-relativistic form overestimates λ by 5% at 10⁵ eV and by 2373% at the top of the range. The regime readout now appends "K ≳ mc²: non-relativistic λ overestimates" above K = 0.05 mc² (verified to fire from log₁₀K = −14 upward, silent below).

Now: Q1 cold-opens on the intuition-answerable wave baseline with a neutral stem, card 2 is an OBSERVE-COMPARE that states no conclusion, cards 3 and 4 are gated predictions whose distractors are named rival mechanisms (accumulation, "the metal only sets their speed"), and card 5 is a naming TELL that introduces the stopping potential. 5 cards, 3 gates, zero UI choreography, every claim verifiable from the visuals.

Verified against the oracle at all 55 grid points.
- FIXED [P2][D8] readout labels now carry each quantity's NAME as well as its symbol ("Threshold frequency f₀", "Max kinetic energy K_max = hf − φ", "Stopping potential V₀"), f₀ gained its missing unit ("5.51 ×10¹⁴ **Hz**"), and the metal list is ordered by increasing φ (Cu 4.70 was listed before Zn 4.30). The longer labels initially crushed the value column — caught by measurement, not by eye: `scrollWidth` 110 vs `clientWidth` 80 on the f₀ row, the 18 px K_max value wrapping to two lines. Fixed with a readout-scoped rule (label wraps, `.stat-value{flex:0 0 auto;width:max-content;white-space:nowrap}`); every row now measures `scrollWidth === clientWidth` and sits inside the box at both viewports.
- FIXED [P2][D8] the lamp's two centred readout lines were clamped to a hard-coded `x ≥ 62` and sat on the canvas's left edge at 1024×768 — the clamp is now measured from `ctx.measureText` (half-width + 10 px). - FIXED [P2][DEMO] the drawn electron speed was `130·√(K + 0.05)` — an affine offset that made a K_max = 0.05 eV electron leave √2 times too fast and gave a K_max = 0 electron 13% of full speed, muting exactly the "K_max → 0 at threshold ⇒ the electron barely escapes" behaviour. Offset cut to 0.004 (K_max = 2 eV still maps to 184 px/s).
- FIXED [P2][LAYER] free exploration inherited the last inquiry card's scene caption; `__freeExplore` now sets its own. `__cgReveals` called `drawScene` but not `updateReadouts`, so the staged sidebar rows stayed dashed when the Controls Guide opened — fixed. Info-modal D6/A2 rows and the Controls-Guide Reset and readouts copy updated to the new behaviour.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — the I–V characteristic and a collector, i.e. the actual experiment the concept is named after.** "Observations" in the JEE sense IS the phototube: a collector plate, a variable retarding/accelerating voltage, an ammeter, and the family of photocurrent-vs-voltage curves that saturate at different heights for different intensities but all cut the voltage axis at the SAME V₀ — the single picture that separates "how many" from "how fast". Sketch: add an anode and a battery symbol to the existing scene, a `Collector voltage` slider (−3 V … +5 V) that decelerates each electron by eV (an electron with K < eV turns back before the anode — visible, and the mechanism behind V₀), a photocurrent readout replacing/beside the ejection rate, and a second canvas plotting I vs V that retains up to three traces with a **fixed** current axis (an auto-fitting axis would cancel the very intensity dependence the plot exists to show). (2) **the V₀-vs-f line — the sim's deepest point, currently only asserted.** Card 5 claims "K_max grows linearly with f, slope h" and the student has no way to see it; a small plot accumulating one point per (metal, f) setting would show the line, let the student read h/e off the slope, and — by switching metals — show the intercept move while the slope does not. The numbers are already exact (measured above); only the representation is missing. (3) **draw the escape**: an electron currently materialises at the plate surface; showing the chosen bound electron absorb the photon, climb the φ barrier and leave with the remainder would make the energy budget a mechanism rather than a bar. (4) **a photon-energy ledger stacked on the electron itself** (φ segment + K segment summing to hf) instead of the bottom-edge bar, which is small and far from the action. (5) **a wave-picture ghost mode** — a side-by-side "what the wave theory predicts" (brighter ⇒ faster electrons, a computable time lag at low intensity) against the observed behaviour, making card 1's refutation experiencable rather than only narrated.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the work functions are literature values rather than NCERT Table 11.1 (sodium 2.28 eV as in Halliday, vs NCERT's 2.75; Cs 2.10 vs 2.14; Ca 2.87 CRC; Cu 4.70) — internally consistent and defensible, but a student checking f₀ against the NCERT table for sodium will get 6.65 ×10¹⁴ rather than 5.51 ×10¹⁴. The frequency→hue map is exact at its endpoints (4.3 ×10¹⁴ red, 7.5 ×10¹⁴ violet) but runs about one band green-ward in the middle (5.0 ×10¹⁴ = 600 nm draws yellow-green rather than orange). `onStep` re-pins the apparatus on every entry to a card, so paging back and forward discards a student's mid-card slider changes — deliberate (the card's stem must stay true), and the pager is the only way to trigger it. The ejection-rate EMA has a ±5% ripple at 100% intensity, inherent to counting a Poisson process over a 0.6 s window. The ⓘ Info modal states the concept and hence card 1's answer, as in every sim in this corpus — opt-in meta-UI. At 1024×768 the sidebar's controls sit below the fold while the inquiry zone is open; that is the template's layout contract (inquiry zone first), not a defect of this file. The only console entry at any grid point, either theme, either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404. Named defect classes checked explicitly: `ctx.font = 'var(--font-sans)'` — **not present** (all eight sites interpolate the resolved computed value; every canvas string 12–13 px at DPR 1); canvas theme from `documentElement` — not present (`css()` already read `document.body`, and the light theme repaints while paused: canvas bg 6,14,17 → 226,232,240 on toggle); predict-gate leak — **present in four places, all fixed**; pause-desync — not present (every control handler reaches `drawScene` via `updateReadouts`; with the sim paused a frequency change repainted the lamp pixel 245,107,61 → 209,194,214); auto-scale cancelling a control — not applicable, the sim has no plot (which is itself ENHANCEMENT 1, and the fixed-axis warning is recorded there); affine/clamped encodings — **one present, fixed** (the √(K+0.05) electron speed); the hf bar's 6 eV reference length is fixed and honest (hf clamps at 6 eV, above the whole reachable range of 1.45–4.96 eV); temporal aliasing — not applicable (no integrator; the photon hit test is swept so a stalled frame cannot skip the plate).

### 2026-09-11 · `Module-14-Modern-Physics/C230-radioactive-decay-law-and-half-life.html` — jee-sim-deep-review (deep, P + D + D8 + L)

- TRACK P: grid **380 points, 0 mismatches** — 5 (τ, N₀) trajectories sampled live from the DOM 362 times (τ=100/N₀=2500, τ=20/N₀=50, τ=20/N₀=5000, τ=400/N₀=2500, and τ=20/N₀=2500 post-fix to run end) plus 18 static readout points (τ ∈ {10,20,60,100,200,400}, N₀ ∈ {50,500,1000,2500,5000}). Decay is stochastic per nucleus, so the oracle is N(t)/N₀ ~ Binomial(N₀, e^(−t/τ))/N₀ with σ = √(N₀p(1−p))/N₀ and the bar is 3σ: **max |z| = 2.69 over all 362 samples, 0 beyond 3σ**. The per-frame draw `p = 1 − exp(−dt/τ)` is exact for any dt (memorylessness), so the Speed control and frame stalls cannot bias the answer — confirmed at 1× and 4×. The JEE-critical claim was verified numerically rather than assumed: **T½ is independent of N₀** — measured crossings of N/N₀ = 1/2 at 13.4 s (N₀=5000) and 16.4 s (N₀=50) against 13.86 s expected, the spread being exactly the √N statistics (σ_T ≈ T½/√N ≈ 2.0 s at N₀=50, 0.2 s at N₀=5000) — and **successive half-lives are equal**: at τ=20 the post-fix run crossed 0.5 / 0.25 / 0.125 at 13.9 / 27.9 / 43.7 s vs 13.86 / 27.73 / 41.59 s. Terminal point 38/2500 at t = 83.2 s vs 39.0 expected (z = −0.16). λ = 1/τ and A = λN reproduce to displayed precision at every grid point. Browser-verified: yes.

- FIXED [P2][D8] **glosses.** The grid caption's "— each dot is one nucleus" was destroyed by the first `updateReadouts()` call (overwritten with "— 2500 nuclei"); it now reads "— 2500 nuclei, one dot each" permanently. The unlabelled reference exponential is now "theory: e^(−t/τ)", and the revealed overlay carries "1/e ≈ 0.37 left at t = τ" instead of a bare "1/e". The Formal panel's `At t = τ (mean lifetime)` label rendered through `text-transform:uppercase` as "AT T = T" — t and τ collapsing to the same glyph in the one row whose subject is their difference — reworded to "After one mean lifetime".
- FIXED [P2][DEMO/PHYS] **the decay constant λ and the activity A did not exist in the sim.** NCERT/JEE state the law as N = N₀e^(−λt) with T½ = 0.693/λ and examine activity A = λN in becquerel; this sim was parameterised only by τ and never wrote λ once, so a student could finish it without meeting the symbol the exam uses. Added to the existing readouts panel: surviving count N ("1827 of 2500"), decay constant λ = 1/τ, activity A = λN in decays/s; `eq1`/`eq2` now carry both parameterisations (`e^(−t/τ) = e^(−λt), λ = 1/τ` and `T½ = τ ln 2 = ln2/λ`) and a fifth Formal row states `A = λN = A₀e^(−λt)` with the Bq gloss. Verified against the oracle at every grid point (λ = 0.0500 s⁻¹ at τ = 20; A = 91.4 decays/s at N = 1827). The three new rows initially wrapped the λ value across two lines — caught by measurement, not by eye (`scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth`) — fixed with a readouts-scoped rule; all five rows now measure `scrollWidth === clientWidth` at both viewports.
- FIXED [P2][DEMO/LAYER] the axis title was a bare "time (s)" for an axis that silently rescales with τ (see the residual below); it now reads "time (s) — axis spans 6 half-lives". Its first wording collided with the "0" and "83 s" tick labels at 1024×768 (both rows sat inside the same 12 px band); `padB` 42 → 54 separates the tick row from the axis title, re-verified at both viewports. Controls-Guide step 8 and the welcome blurb/voiceover were updated to match the new readouts and to stop previewing card 2's mechanism ("the smooth curve is a crowd of coin flips" → the memorylessness statement).
- **DEMONSTRATION defect, MITIGATED not fixed — τ is pixel-dead on the plot [P1][DEMO].** Both axes are normalised to τ (x to 6 T½, y to N/N₀), so the plotted picture is τ-invariant by construction, including the τ marker itself, which sits at a fixed 1/(6 ln 2) = 0.24 of the width at every τ. Measured at t = 0, paused: sweeping τ from 20 s to 10 s changes **87 of 437 157 pixels (0.020%)**, and the changed region is the bounding box [529,738]–[542,748] — the "83 s"→"42 s" tick label and nothing else; τ = 100 and 400 add ~1500 px of sub-pixel antialiasing on the dashed verticals and no visible change. (N₀ is likewise **exactly 0 px** on the plot at t = 0, but that one is the lesson — the raggedness the normalised axis exposes *is* card 2 — and it changes 205 804 px of the grid.) The fix inside a surgical budget would have to either pin the axis in absolute seconds — which at a 40× τ range leaves the curve either finished in the left 3% or not reaching one half-life — or add a representation, so the honest move was to state the normalisation on the axis (done, above) and file the real fix as ENHANCEMENT 1. Recorded here as a defect, not as a residual.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — hold-and-compare two lifetimes on one absolute-time axis.** This is the fix for the pixel-dead τ, and it is also the missing *essential contrast* of the topic: a "Hold this curve" button freezes the current (τ, N₀) trace as a ghost, and the plot switches to absolute seconds spanning 6 T½ of the **larger** of the two τ values, so a τ = 20 s and a τ = 60 s sample sit on one axis — the short-lived one plummeting, the long-lived one still above half when the other is at 1/64. Every ratio JEE asks about (what fraction is left after a given *absolute* time, which sample is "hotter" at t = 0 versus t = 3 T½) becomes readable off the picture instead of off two remembered pictures. Sketch: `held = {tau, N0, history}` on the button, `tMaxAbs = 6·ln2·max(τ, τ_held)`, ghost trace at 45% alpha with its own τ tag, the existing T½ markers drawn for both; the normalised view stays the default so nothing about cards 1–5 changes. (2) **plot the activity A(t) on a second y-axis** (or as a second trace in units of A₀): the sim now *reports* A = λN but never shows that the activity curve is the N curve — same half-life, different vertical scale — which is exactly the exam trap "does the activity halve in one half-life?". The data is already in `state.history`. (3) **a semi-log toggle.** One checkbox turns the exponential into the straight line whose slope is −λ, which is how every real decay measurement is read and is the cleanest demonstration that a *single* decay constant governs the whole run; on a small-N₀ run the scatter about the straight line is also the most legible form of the √N lesson. (4) **a per-half-life count ledger** — a small table filled in as each T½ marker is crossed (N₀, N₀/2, N₀/4 …, measured against predicted), turning the sim's central claim into a record the student accumulates rather than a shape they squint at. (5) **daughter accumulation**: draw the decayed dots as a second species and plot N_daughter = N₀ − N, so that "nothing disappears, it transmutes" and N_p + N_d = N₀ are visible — the standard follow-on question and one extra trace.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): the 6-T½ / N/N₀ normalisation is deliberate and is disclosed in the ⓘ modal (principle J5) and now on the axis itself; it is what makes the exponential's self-similarity visible across parameters, and its cost is ENHANCEMENT 1. `window.__audit` is weak — `at()` re-calls the same closed form the sim displays, and the `equalTimeIntervalsRemoveEqualFractions` invariant compares `Engine.pdata.survivingFraction` with itself and never touches `state`, so it cannot fail; the real verification here is the external binomial oracle above. The sim never draws a decay *event* (a dot changes colour between frames rather than emitting anything) — correct for a law-of-large-numbers demo, and the α/β/γ mechanism belongs to C229. History is decimated above 4000 points, which thins the staircase on very long runs by design. Named defect classes checked explicitly: a stated convention/formula the code does not obey — **none**, every displayed identity (T½ = τ ln 2, 1/e at t = τ, (1/2)ⁿ, λ = 1/τ, A = λN) matches the code and the oracle; drawn geometry encoding wrong physics — none, the trace is the literal survivor count and the dashed curve is the exact exponential; a card teaching an invalid mechanism — none (card 2's rebuttals name the right cause, statistical fluctuation ∝ √N); `ctx.font = 'var(--font-sans)'` — not present; canvas theme from `documentElement` — **present in both draw functions, fixed**; predict-gate leaks — **two found (slider caption, τ/1-e overlay), both fixed**; welcome hero/blurb checked (hero label is `display:none`; blurb reworded); pause-desync — **not present**, with the loop stopped a τ or N₀ drag repaints both canvases and the sidebar agrees (paused, τ→200: t 0.0 s, N 2500/2500, λ 0.0050 s⁻¹, A 12.5 decays/s, caption T½ = 138.6 s); a control that cannot be toggled back — none, Formal/Maximize/Guided Inquiry/Controls Guide/theme all round-trip and the four `data-no-reset` shields hold (τ and elapsed time survived every toggle); Reset preserves the student's slider values (tau=200, N₀=1000 kept) as the Controls-Guide copy claims; slider step too coarse — no critical value exists in this physics, and N₀ = 50 (the value card 3 names) is exactly reachable; normalised/auto-fitting axes — **present, measured, reported above**; affine/clamped encodings — none; temporal aliasing — not applicable, the per-nucleus draw is exact for any dt. The only console entry at any grid point, either theme, either viewport, is the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404.
- Gates: syntax ✓ (7/7 inline blocks) · jsdom ×3 ✓ (welcome-overlay PASS · guided-inquiry 5 cards PASS · controls-tutorial 9 steps PASS — run without `--baseline`, no git operations permitted in this loop) · user-version-eval ✓ · layout-probe ✓ · canvas-check — the repo script sweeps a hardcoded QM directory and does not cover this file, so the backing-store:display ratio was compared against the pre-fix measurement directly: `#grid-canvas` and `#plot-canvas` both 567×771 on 567×771 (1.000 / 1.000) at 1500×950 and 329×550 on 329×550 at 1024×768, identical to baseline ✓ · copyright ✓
- S3: re-push required for this file.

  - **COMPLETED** (the parts the killed agent left half-done): its legend recolour to `#64748b` fixed the legend's own contrast but silently broke legend↔canvas agreement — finished by moving the canvas ink to the same value; its light-theme legend background rule left the gold overlay on the same surface at 1.6:1 — finished; its `padB`/label work left the τ tick an unglossed Greek letter — finished.
  - **REVERTED: none.** Every inherited edit survived independent verification.
- MEASURED, REPORTED, NOT FIXED (the brief's two named measurements): **the time axis auto-fits to the half-life, so τ is pixel-dead in the plot.** `tMax = 6·ln2·τ` and `xOf(t) = t/tMax`, which cancels τ exactly. Hashed the plot interior (excluding the tick-label rows) at t = 0: byte-identical `31722be2` for τ = 20, 50, 100, 200 and 400 — a 20× change in the sim's central parameter moves **zero** interior pixels. Only the right tick label (83 s → 1663 s), the `T½ =` caption and the decay *rate* respond. This is deliberate (ⓘ modal principle J5: self-similarity of the exponential) and is now disclosed on the axis itself, in the caption and in the sidebar, and the 8×/16× speeds make the rate difference watchable — the real cure is ENHANCEMENT 1. **The N axis also normalises to N/N₀**, but N₀ is *not* dead: the sample canvas is honest (non-background pixel count 114234 → 153186 → 168699 → 186944 for N₀ = 50/500/2500/5000) and on the plot N₀ shows as statistical raggedness — 76% of the drawn trace columns are flat runs (visible single-nucleus steps) at N₀ = 50, which is exactly what card 3 claims.
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — hold-and-compare two lifetimes on one absolute-time axis.** The cure for the pixel-dead τ and the topic's missing essential contrast: a "Hold this curve" button freezes the current (τ, N₀) trace as a ghost and the plot switches to absolute seconds spanning 6·T½ of the *larger* τ, so a 20 s and a 60 s sample sit on one axis — one plummeting while the other is still above half. Sketch: `held = {tau, N0, history}`, `tMaxAbs = 6·ln2·max(τ, τ_held)`, ghost at 45% alpha with its own τ tag, T½ markers for both; the normalised view stays the default so cards 1–5 are untouched. (2) **plot A(t) as a second trace in units of A₀** — the sim now reports A = λN but never shows that the activity curve *is* the N curve with a different vertical scale, which is the exam trap "does activity halve in one half-life?"; the data is already in `state.history`. (3) **a semi-log toggle** — one checkbox turns the exponential into the straight line of slope −λ, how every real decay measurement is read, and on a small-N₀ run the scatter about that line is the most legible form of the √N lesson. (4) **a per-half-life count ledger** filled in as each T½ marker is crossed (N₀, N₀/2, N₀/4 …, measured vs predicted). (5) **daughter accumulation** — draw decayed dots as a second species and plot N_d = N₀ − N so "nothing disappears, it transmutes" and N + N_d = N₀ are visible.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): `window.__audit` is self-referential — `at()` re-calls the same closed form the sim displays and the `equalTimeIntervalsRemoveEqualFractions` invariant compares `Engine.pdata.survivingFraction` with itself without touching `state`, so it cannot fail; the external binomial oracle above is the real verification. The sim draws no decay *event* (a dot changes state between frames) — correct for a law-of-large-numbers demo; the α/β/γ mechanism belongs to C229. History is decimated above 4000 points by design. `renderMath()` retries on a bare 50 ms `setTimeout` with no attempt cap (house pattern across the wave; KaTeX resolved on every run here). Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: wrong argument shape at a library call; drawn geometry encoding wrong physics (the trace is the literal survivor count, the dashed reference is the exact exponential); a card teaching an invalid mechanism (card 2's rebuttals name statistical fluctuation ∝ √N, which is the right cause); `ctx.font = 'var(--…)'`; any canvas string under 12px; pause-desync (paused, N₀ → 300: canvas repainted and the sidebar read "300 of 300" in agreement); `restartOnParamChange` reverting a sibling control (only two sliders exist and both are snapshot-restored; the `data-no-reset` shields on `#btn-gi`, `#btn-cg`, the welcome overlay and the guide nav all hold, and the Feedback pill now carries one too); a control that cannot be toggled back; a slider step too coarse (no critical τ exists, and N₀ = 50 — the value card 3 names — is exactly reachable at step 50); affine/clamped encodings; temporal aliasing (the per-nucleus draw is exact for any dt); `putImageData` (not used). Predict-gate sweep at both gated cards found nothing leaking in readouts, badges, plot title, legend chips, card stems, Controls-Guide steps, the welcome blurb, the narration or the welcome hero, and `onStep` advances no parameter into the answer state. Console at every grid point, both themes, both viewports: the static server's `/favicon.ico` 404 only.

  - *Kept after independent verification:* the **off-strip suppression** (`onStrip`, edge arrow + count) — re-derived the off-axis set independently and it is exactly the five pairs named, 5 → 4 (4052 nm), 6 → 4 (2626), 6 → 5 (7460), 7 → 5 (4654), 7 → 6 (12 372), with markers suppressed rather than pinned; the **`validateSelections(changed)` rewrite** — 7 pairing cases all honour the select the student touched and leave a legal pair (`EMI From=4` on 7→1 now gives 4→1, where the old code snapped back to 7→1); the **mode-mirroring** in `updateModeUI`; the **speed range 1× → 2×**, measured honest at 7072/3561/1786/1215/904 ms for 0.25/0.5/1/1.5/2× (exactly 1/speed) with λ = 656.5 nm at every setting; the **`.btn-primary:disabled !important`** (computed style during a run: `rgba(30,77,58,0.28)`, `#64748b`, `not-allowed`); **`#aside-inquiry{flex:0 0 auto}`** (`#inq-cards` scrollHeight = clientHeight = 142, no 2 px collapse); the **`#panel-info` promotion above the series block**; the **canvas gloss labels** (`Eₙ = −13.6/n² eV · n = principal quantum number`, `E = 0 — ionisation limit (n → ∞)`, `rings evenly spaced · true Bohr radii rₙ ∝ n²` — the last is the required not-to-scale disclosure); the **45° n-label ladder**; the **font bumps** (every canvas string now ≥ 12 px, verified by reading all 17 `ctxFont*` call sites); the **card 4/5/6 rewrites** and the matching **voice substitutions** for `n₁²`, `n₂²`, `10⁷`, `m⁻¹`; the **About-panel copy** (its "solid = visible, dashed = UV or IR" claim was false when written and is true now).
- ENHANCEMENTS (not applied, ROI-ranked): (1) **highest ROI — a two-strip emission/absorption comparison.** The topic's one essential contrast is that the two spectra are complements, and the sim makes the student hold one in memory while toggling to the other. Sketch: draw both strips stacked (emission above, absorption below) sharing one λ axis and one set of tick gridlines, each fed by its own `lines` array, with a thin vertical tie-line dropped through both at the last-fired λ; `drawStripCommon` already takes `(rect, label, lines, isAbsorption)`, so this is a second call with a halved `stripH` and a taller `totalStripArea`, not a new renderer. (2) **A series-limit / ionisation reach.** `maxN = 7` means the ionisation limit is drawn (E = 0) and named (n → ∞) but unreachable, so the Lyman limit at 91.2 nm and the Balmer limit at 364.6 nm — both examined — cannot be shown; add an `n = ∞` option to both selects that feeds `energyForN(Infinity) = 0`. (3) **A "same gap, both arrows" overlay**: hold the last emission arrow as a ghost while the absorption arrow is drawn, so the up/down pair sits on one ladder. (4) **A compact two-column Last-transition tile** plus a collapsible hint box — the control panel overflows its viewport slot by 359 px at 1500×950 and 541 px at 1024×768 in Guided Inquiry, so Speed, Clear and the status bar are below the fold and card 3's "Read Last transition" needs a scroll at 1024. (5) **Photon-energy-to-scale arrow thickness or a ΔE bar**, so the Lyman/Balmer/Paschen energy ordering is visible without reading numbers.
- Residual (accepted, not defects): UV emission lines are drawn indigo and IR salmon rather than in a single non-spectral ink — the "invisible" claim is carried by the dashed body, the scrimmed band, the `UV — invisible` / `IR — invisible` plates, the on-canvas key and the Region readout, so colour is not the only channel; the orbit rings are equally spaced rather than ∝ n², disclosed on the canvas and in the About panel; the Lyman lines crowd into ~75 px at the series limit, which is the physics, and the label-spacing rule suppresses the labels that would collide; `welcome-fringes` has a 1280×240 backing store stretched to 1500×140 (ratio 0.853/1.714), **identical at HEAD and in every sibling** — a decorative, `aria-hidden` hero canvas, left as the house pattern; the shell's `LISTEN`/`AUTO` micro-labels are 9–10 px, shell chrome shared by 100+ sims. Named defect classes checked explicitly and NOT present: wrong argument shape at a library call; `ctx.font = 'var(--…)'` (no `var()` in any font string); canvas theme read from `documentElement` (the canvas palette is unconditional and the sim ships no theme toggle); pause-desync (no transport; `dt` clamped to 0.05 s and the scene is redrawn every frame); `restartOnParamChange` reverting a sibling control (`data-no-reset` holds on `#btn-gi` and `#btn-cg`); markers clamped to the canvas edge (off-strip lines are suppressed and counted, not pinned); auto-fit cancelling a control (the λ axis is a fixed 50–2500 nm decade window, never rescaled); affine/clamped encodings; temporal aliasing. Predict-gate sweep at both gated cards (2 and 5) found nothing leaking in the readouts, the status bar, the strip, the card stems, the Controls-Guide steps, the welcome blurb or the narration — `onStep` only pins the level pair and fires nothing. (One apparent boot-time leak — a populated `Last transition` panel at card 1 — was traced to a stale headless Chrome still owning the CDP port and **retracted**; on a clean browser every row reads `—`.)
